josshhz11: i have my application where i split up the frontend from the backend into 2 distinct folders, where i will be deploying my backend on render, and my frontend on vercel.

having uploaded this entire folder structure onto github as a new repository, how do i connect both the /frontend to vercel and the /backend to render. can you give me the step by step instructions for how to do so?

GitHub Copilot: I'll guide you through connecting your frontend to Vercel and backend to Render. Here's the step-by-step process:

## Setting Up Vercel for Frontend

### 1. Connect to Vercel
1. Go to [vercel.com](https://vercel.com) and sign in with your GitHub account
2. Click **"New Project"**
3. Import your GitHub repository
4. Select your repository from the list

### 2. Configure Frontend Build Settings
1. **Framework Preset**: Select "Create React App"
2. **Root Directory**: Set to frontend
3. **Build Command**: `npm run build` (should auto-detect)
4. **Output Directory**: `build` (should auto-detect)
5. **Install Command**: `npm install` (should auto-detect)

### 3. Environment Variables (if needed)
1. In the Vercel dashboard, go to your project settings
2. Navigate to **Environment Variables**
3. Add any frontend environment variables (like API URLs)

### 4. Deploy
Click **"Deploy"** and Vercel will build and deploy your frontend

## Setting Up Render for Backend

### 1. Connect to Render
1. Go to [render.com](https://render.com) and sign in with your GitHub account
2. Click **"New"** → **"Web Service"**
3. Connect your GitHub repository

### 2. Configure Backend Settings
1. **Name**: Give your service a name (e.g., "ntu-automator-backend")
2. **Root Directory**: Set to backend
3. **Environment**: Select **"Python 3"**
4. **Build Command**: 
   ```bash
   pip install -r requirements.txt
   ```
5. **Start Command**:
   ```bash
   python app.py
   ```

### 3. Environment Variables
In the Render dashboard, add these environment variables:
- `REDIS_HOST`: Your Redis host
- `REDIS_PORT`: Your Redis port  
- `REDIS_PASSWORD`: Your Redis password
- `SECRET_KEY`: Your secret key
- Any other environment variables your app needs

### 4. Create requirements.txt
Make sure you have a `requirements.txt` file in your backend directory:

````python
fastapi==0.104.1
uvicorn==0.24.0
redis==5.0.1
selenium==4.15.2
python-dotenv==1.0.0
python-multipart==0.0.6
jinja2==3.1.2
aiofiles==23.2.1
starlette==0.27.0
````

## Update Frontend API Configuration

Update your frontend API configuration to point to your Render backend:

````javascript
const API_BASE_URL = process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' 
  ? 'https://your-render-app-name.onrender.com'  // Replace with your Render URL
  : 'http://localhost:5000';

export default API_BASE_URL;
````

## Update Backend CORS Configuration

Update your backend CORS settings to include your Vercel domain:

````python
// ...existing code...
app.add_middleware(
    CORSMiddleware,
    allow_origins=[
        "http://localhost:3000", # React dev server (port 3000)
        "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app", # Vercel frontend domain
        "https://your-vercel-app.vercel.app", # Add your actual Vercel URL
        "https://www.ntu-add-drop-automator.site" # Custom domain name
    ],
    allow_credentials=True, # Important for session cookies
    allow_methods=["*"],
    allow_headers=["*"],
)
// ...existing code...
````

## Important Notes

### For Render:
- **Free tier limitations**: Services sleep after 15 minutes of inactivity
- **Chrome/ChromeDriver**: You'll need to update your Chrome paths for Linux deployment
- **Port configuration**: Render expects your app to run on `PORT` environment variable

### Update your backend for production:

````python
// ...existing code...
def main():
    """Start the server when running directly."""
    port = int(os.environ.get("PORT", 5000))  # Use PORT from environment
    print(f"Starting server on http://0.0.0.0:{port}")
    uvicorn.run(app, host="0.0.0.0", port=port)
// ...existing code...
````

### For Chrome on Render (Linux):
````python
// ...existing code...
# Configure ChromeDriver settings
CHROME_BINARY_PATH = "/usr/bin/google-chrome"  # Linux path for Render
CHROMEDRIVER_PATH = "/usr/local/bin/chromedriver"  # Linux path for Render
// ...existing code...
````

## Final Steps

1. **Test locally** first to ensure everything works
2. **Deploy to both platforms**
3. **Update your frontend** with the correct Render backend URL
4. **Test the deployed applications** to ensure they communicate properly
5. **Set up custom domains** if desired (optional)

Both platforms will automatically redeploy when you push changes to your GitHub repository's respective directories.

josshhz11: is there a reason why i can't access my directory like this?

from https://github.com/josshhz11/ntu-add-drop-automator-v2/backend

but i can access it only through this:
https://github.com/josshhz11/ntu-add-drop-automator-v2/tree/main/backend

on Render i can't direct to either of them and it ends up defaulting to this:
https://github.com/josshhz11/ntu-add-drop-automator-v2/

which doesn't have the Dockerfile so that's an issue. how do i fix this?

GitHub Copilot: The issue you're experiencing is due to how GitHub URLs work and how deployment platforms like Render handle repository connections. Here's what's happening and how to fix it:

## Why the URLs work differently:

1. **backend** - This format doesn't exist on GitHub. GitHub doesn't support direct folder access via URLs like this.

2. **`/tree/main/backend`** - This is the correct GitHub URL format for browsing folders in the web interface.

3. **Render defaults to root** - When you connect a repository to Render, it clones the entire repository and starts from the root directory by default.

## Solutions:

### Option 1: Add Dockerfile to Backend Directory (Recommended)

Create a `Dockerfile` in your backend directory:

````dockerfile
FROM python:3.11-slim

# Install Chrome dependencies
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y \
    wget \
    gnupg \
    unzip \
    curl \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Install Chrome
RUN wget -q -O - https://dl-ssl.google.com/linux/linux_signing_key.pub | apt-key add - \
    && echo "deb [arch=amd64] http://dl.google.com/linux/chrome/deb/ stable main" >> /etc/apt/sources.list.d/google.list \
    && apt-get update \
    && apt-get install -y google-chrome-stable \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Install ChromeDriver
RUN CHROMEDRIVER_VERSION=`curl -sS chromedriver.storage.googleapis.com/LATEST_RELEASE` \
    && wget -N http://chromedriver.storage.googleapis.com/$CHROMEDRIVER_VERSION/chromedriver_linux64.zip \
    && unzip chromedriver_linux64.zip \
    && rm chromedriver_linux64.zip \
    && mv chromedriver /usr/local/bin/chromedriver \
    && chmod +x /usr/local/bin/chromedriver

WORKDIR /app

COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

COPY . .

EXPOSE 5000

CMD ["python", "app.py"]
````

### Option 2: Configure Render Settings Properly

When setting up your service on Render:

1. **Repository**: Select your repository normally
2. **Root Directory**: Set to backend (this tells Render to use the backend folder as the root)
3. **Environment**: Docker (if using Dockerfile) or Python 3
4. **Build Command**: 
   ```bash
   pip install -r requirements.txt
   ```
5. **Start Command**:
   ```bash
   python app.py
   ```

### Option 3: Update Your App.py for Production

Make sure your app is configured for production deployment:

````python
# ...existing code...

def main():
    """Start the server when running directly."""
    port = int(os.environ.get("PORT", 5000))  # Render provides PORT env var
    print(f"Starting server on http://0.0.0.0:{port}")
    uvicorn.run(app, host="0.0.0.0", port=port)

# ...existing code...
````

### Option 4: Create Requirements.txt

Ensure you have a proper `requirements.txt` in your backend directory:

````text
fastapi==0.104.1
uvicorn[standard]==0.24.0
redis==5.0.1
selenium==4.15.2
python-dotenv==1.0.0
python-multipart==0.0.6
jinja2==3.1.2
aiofiles==23.2.1
starlette==0.27.0
pydantic==2.5.0
````

### Recommended Render Configuration:

1. **Service Name**: `ntu-automator-backend`
2. **Root Directory**: backend
3. **Environment**: `Docker` (if using Dockerfile) or `Python 3`
4. **Branch**: `main`
5. **Build Command**: `pip install -r requirements.txt`
6. **Start Command**: `python app.py`

### Environment Variables to Set on Render:

- `REDIS_HOST`: Your Redis instance URL
- `REDIS_PORT`: `6379`
- `REDIS_PASSWORD`: Your Redis password
- `SECRET_KEY`: A secure random string

The key is setting the **Root Directory** to backend in Render's settings, which tells it to treat the backend folder as the project root instead of looking for files in the repository root.

josshhz11: but i can't find it in Render. do you know why?

it only finds the main repository

GitHub Copilot: The issue is that Render is looking for the repository name as a subdirectory, but GitHub repositories don't work that way. Here are the correct steps to fix this:

## Solution: Use the Full Repository and Configure Root Directory

### 1. Connect the Main Repository
1. In Render, search for just: `ntu-add-drop-automator-v2`
2. Click **"Connect"** on your main repository

### 2. Configure the Root Directory
After connecting, you'll see the service configuration page. Set:
- **Root Directory**: backend
- **Build Command**: `pip install -r requirements.txt`
- **Start Command**: `python app.py`

### 3. Update Your App for Render's PORT Environment Variable

Your current app uses port 5000, but Render expects you to use the PORT environment variable:

````python
// ...existing code...
def main():
    """Start the server when running directly."""
    port = int(os.environ.get("PORT", 5000))  # Use Render's PORT env var
    print(f"Starting server on http://0.0.0.0:{port}")
    uvicorn.run(app, host="0.0.0.0", port=port)
// ...existing code...
````

### 4. Create a requirements.txt file

Make sure you have this in your backend directory:

````text
fastapi==0.104.1
uvicorn[standard]==0.24.0
redis==5.0.1
selenium==4.15.2
python-dotenv==1.0.0
python-multipart==0.0.6
jinja2==3.1.2
aiofiles==23.2.1
starlette==0.27.0
pydantic==2.5.0
````

### 5. Add Chrome Installation for Linux

Since Render uses Linux, you'll need to update your Chrome paths and potentially add a build script. Create a `render-build.sh`:

````bash
#!/bin/bash

# Install Chrome dependencies
apt-get update
apt-get install -y wget gnupg unzip curl

# Install Chrome
wget -q -O - https://dl-ssl.google.com/linux/linux_signing_key.pub | apt-key add -
echo "deb [arch=amd64] http://dl.google.com/linux/chrome/deb/ stable main" >> /etc/apt/sources.list.d/google.list
apt-get update
apt-get install -y google-chrome-stable

# Install ChromeDriver
CHROMEDRIVER_VERSION=$(curl -sS chromedriver.storage.googleapis.com/LATEST_RELEASE)
wget -N http://chromedriver.storage.googleapis.com/$CHROMEDRIVER_VERSION/chromedriver_linux64.zip
unzip chromedriver_linux64.zip
rm chromedriver_linux64.zip
mv chromedriver /usr/local/bin/chromedriver
chmod +x /usr/local/bin/chromedriver

# Install Python dependencies
pip install -r requirements.txt
````

Then update your Render configuration:
- **Build Command**: `bash render-build.sh`
- **Start Command**: `python app.py`

### Alternative: Use Docker (Recommended)

Create a Dockerfile in your backend directory:

````dockerfile
FROM python:3.11-slim

# Install Chrome and dependencies
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y \
    wget \
    gnupg \
    unzip \
    curl \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Install Chrome
RUN wget -q -O - https://dl-ssl.google.com/linux/linux_signing_key.pub | apt-key add - \
    && echo "deb [arch=amd64] http://dl.google.com/linux/chrome/deb/ stable main" >> /etc/apt/sources.list.d/google.list \
    && apt-get update \
    && apt-get install -y google-chrome-stable \
    && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Install ChromeDriver
RUN CHROMEDRIVER_VERSION=`curl -sS chromedriver.storage.googleapis.com/LATEST_RELEASE` \
    && wget -N http://chromedriver.storage.googleapis.com/$CHROMEDRIVER_VERSION/chromedriver_linux64.zip \
    && unzip chromedriver_linux64.zip \
    && rm chromedriver_linux64.zip \
    && mv chromedriver /usr/local/bin/chromedriver \
    && chmod +x /usr/local/bin/chromedriver

WORKDIR /app

COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

COPY . .

EXPOSE 5000

CMD ["python", "app.py"]
````

Then in Render:
- **Environment**: Docker
- **Root Directory**: backend
- **Docker Command**: (leave blank, it will use the CMD from Dockerfile)

### Key Points:
- **Don't search for subdirectories** - Render can't find `repository/backend`
- **Use the main repository** and set **Root Directory** to backend
- **Update your code** to use Render's PORT environment variable
- **Consider using Docker** for easier Chrome/ChromeDriver setup

This should resolve the issue and allow Render to find and deploy your backend properly.

GitHub Copilot: Looking at your logs, I can see several issues. Let me help you fix them:

## 1. Port Configuration Issue

**Problem**: Render is detecting port 5000, but you should use Render's `PORT` environment variable.

**Fix**: Update your `main()` function in app.py:

````python
// ...existing code...
def main():
    """Start the server when running directly."""
    port = int(os.environ.get("PORT", 5000))  # Use Render's PORT env var
    print(f"Starting server on http://0.0.0.0:{port}")
    uvicorn.run(app, host="0.0.0.0", port=port)
// ...existing code...
````

## 2. Missing Root Route (404 Error)

**Problem**: Your app has no root route (`/`), causing 404 errors.

**Fix**: Add a root route in your FastAPI app:

````python
// ...existing code...
    # Add this route after your other routes
    @app.get('/')
    async def root():
        return {"message": "NTU Add-Drop Automator Backend API", "status": "running"}

    @app.get('/health')
    async def health_check():
        return {"status": "healthy", "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat()}
// ...existing code...
````

## 3. Frontend API Configuration Issue

**Problem**: Your frontend is likely calling the wrong API URL.

**Fix**: Update your API configuration:

````javascript
const API_BASE_URL = process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' 
  ? 'https://ntu-add-drop-automator-v2-backend.onrender.com'  // Your Render URL
  : 'http://localhost:5000';

export default API_BASE_URL;
````

## 4. CORS Configuration Update

**Fix**: Update your CORS settings with your actual Vercel domain:

````python
// ...existing code...
    # CORS configuration for React development
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000", # React dev server (port 3000)
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-v2.vercel.app", # Update with your actual Vercel URL
            "https://your-vercel-domain.vercel.app", # Add your actual Vercel domain here
            "https://www.ntu-add-drop-automator.site" # Custom domain name
        ],
        allow_credentials=True, # Important for session cookies
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
// ...existing code...
````

## 5. Add Logout Route

**Problem**: Your frontend is calling `/api/logout/{swap_id}` but it doesn't exist.

**Fix**: Add the missing logout route:

````python
// ...existing code...
    # API route for logout/cleanup
    @app.post('/api/logout/{swap_id}')
    async def api_logout(swap_id: str, request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            # Clean up session
            request.session.clear()
            
            # Clean up Redis data
            redis_db.delete(swap_id)
            
            return {"success": True, "message": "Logged out successfully"}
        except Exception as e:
            # Even if cleanup fails, consider logout successful
            return {"success": True, "message": "Logged out"}
// ...existing code...
````

## 6. Environment Variables on Render

Make sure you've set these environment variables in your Render dashboard:

- `REDIS_HOST`: Your Redis host
- `REDIS_PORT`: `6379`
- `REDIS_PASSWORD`: Your Redis password  
- `SECRET_KEY`: A secure random string

## 7. Check Your Vercel Domain

To find your actual Vercel domain:

1. Go to your Vercel dashboard
2. Click on your project
3. Copy the domain from the deployment URL
4. Update the CORS settings and frontend API config

## 8. Test the Backend

Once deployed, test your backend by visiting:
- `https://ntu-add-drop-automator-v2-backend.onrender.com/` (should return the root message)
- `https://ntu-add-drop-automator-v2-backend.onrender.com/health` (should return health status)

## 9. Common Issues to Check

**Session/Cookie Issues**: Since you're using sessions, make sure:
- Your frontend and backend domains are configured correctly
- `withCredentials: true` is set in all your axios calls
- CORS allows credentials

**Render Free Tier**: Remember that Render's free tier:
- Goes to sleep after 15 minutes of inactivity
- Takes 30+ seconds to wake up on first request

After making these changes, redeploy both your backend and frontend, then test the login flow again.

GitHub Copilot: 

josshhz11: for my backend on render, i know that when a change is made and pushed to the github repo, the whole thing is rebuilt and redeployed.

however, for the frontend on vercel, when i make changes to the code and push the changes to github, does it redeploy? how do i configure it to?

GitHub Copilot: Yes, Vercel automatically redeploys when you push changes to GitHub, but you need to make sure it's properly configured. Here's how to set it up and verify:

## Vercel Auto-Deployment Configuration

### 1. Check Git Integration
In your Vercel dashboard:
1. Go to your project settings
2. Click on **"Git"** in the sidebar
3. Verify that:
   - **Production Branch** is set to `main` (or your default branch)
   - **Auto-deploy** is enabled for the production branch

### 2. Configure Build Settings
Make sure your build settings are correct:
1. In Vercel dashboard → **Settings** → **General**
2. **Framework Preset**: `Create React App`
3. **Root Directory**: frontend (since your frontend is in a subdirectory)
4. **Build Command**: `npm run build`
5. **Output Directory**: `build`
6. **Install Command**: `npm install`

### 3. Environment Variables for Production
Add environment variables in Vercel:
1. Go to **Settings** → **Environment Variables**
2. Add any production environment variables your app needs

### 4. Update Your API Configuration

Your current API config looks correct, but make sure you're using it properly in your components:

````javascript
const API_BASE_URL = process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' 
  ? 'https://ntu-add-drop-automator-v2-backend.onrender.com'
  : 'http://localhost:5000';

export default API_BASE_URL;
````

### 5. Use the API_BASE_URL in Your Components

Make sure you're importing and using this in your components:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
import API_BASE_URL from '../../config/api';

const HomePage = () => {
  // ...existing code...
  
  const handleSubmit = async (event) => {
    event.preventDefault();
    setLoading(true);
    setMessage('');

    try {
      const response = await fetch(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/login`, {
        method: 'POST',
        headers: {
          'Content-Type': 'application/json',
        },
        credentials: 'include', // Important for cookies/sessions
        body: JSON.stringify(formData)
      });

      // ...rest of your code...
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Error:', error);
      setMessage('Network error occurred');
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };
  // ...existing code...
};
````

### 6. Verify Auto-Deployment

To test if auto-deployment works:
1. Make a small change to your frontend code
2. Push to your GitHub repository
3. Go to your Vercel dashboard
4. You should see a new deployment being built in the **Deployments** tab

### 7. Check Deployment Logs

If auto-deployment isn't working:
1. Go to **Deployments** tab in Vercel
2. Click on the latest deployment
3. Check the **Build Logs** for any errors

### 8. Common Issues and Fixes

**Issue**: Vercel not detecting changes
**Fix**: 
- Ensure you're pushing to the correct branch
- Check that the Git integration is properly connected

**Issue**: Build failures
**Fix**: 
- Check build logs for specific errors
- Ensure `package.json` is in the frontend directory
- Verify all dependencies are listed in `package.json`

**Issue**: Environment variables not working
**Fix**:
- Make sure environment variables are set in Vercel dashboard
- Restart the deployment after adding new env vars

### 9. Manual Redeploy (if needed)

If auto-deployment isn't working, you can manually trigger a redeploy:
1. Go to your Vercel project dashboard
2. Click **"Redeploy"** on the latest deployment
3. Select **"Use existing Build Cache"** or **"Rebuild"**

### 10. Webhook Configuration (Advanced)

If you need more control, you can set up webhooks:
1. Go to **Settings** → **Git**
2. Configure deploy hooks for specific branches or events

Your Vercel URL from the screenshot is: `ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app`

josshhz11: how do i know if this is a backend/frontend issue? how do i debug this? 

this message i keep getting even though the credentials are correct are from here:
try {
      // Validate that num_modules is a positive number
      const numModules = parseInt(formData.num_modules);
      if (numModules < 1 || numModules > 6) { // Add reasonable limits
        setMessage('Please enter a valid number of modules (1-6)');
        setLoading(false);
        return;
      }
      
      // Create FormData for the API call
      const formDataToSend = new FormData();
      formDataToSend.append('username', formData.username);
      formDataToSend.append('password', formData.password);
      formDataToSend.append('num_modules', formData.num_modules);

      // Call the login backend API
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/login`, formDataToSend, {
        headers: {
          'Content-Type': 'multipart/form-data',
        },
        withCredentials: true, // Important for session cookies
      });

      if (response.data.success) {
        // Navigate to the input-index page on the frontend
        navigate('/input-index', {
          state: {
            num_modules: numModules,
            username: formData.username // do we need to pass this in?
          }
        });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Login error:', error);
      const errorMessage = error.response?.data?.detail || 'Login failed. Please try again.'; 
      setMessage(errorMessage);
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }

GitHub Copilot: Here's how to debug and identify if this is a frontend or backend issue:

## 1. Check Browser Developer Tools

Open your browser's Developer Tools (F12) and check:

### Network Tab:
1. Open Network tab before submitting the form
2. Submit the form and look for the `/api/login` request
3. Check:
   - **Status Code**: 200 (success), 4xx (client error), 5xx (server error)
   - **Response**: Click on the request to see the actual response
   - **Request Headers**: Verify the correct URL is being called
   - **Request Payload**: Verify the form data is being sent correctly

### Console Tab:
Look for any JavaScript errors or the `console.error('Login error:', error);` output.

## 2. Add Debug Logging to Frontend

Update your HomePage.js to add more detailed logging:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  const handleSubmit = async (event) => {
    event.preventDefault();
    setLoading(true);
    setMessage('')

    try {
      // Validate that num_modules is a positive number
      const numModules = parseInt(formData.num_modules);
      if (numModules < 1 || numModules > 6) {
        setMessage('Please enter a valid number of modules (1-6)');
        setLoading(false);
        return;
      }
      
      // Debug: Log the API URL being used
      console.log('API_BASE_URL:', API_BASE_URL);
      console.log('Full URL:', `${API_BASE_URL}/api/login`);
      
      // Create FormData for the API call
      const formDataToSend = new FormData();
      formDataToSend.append('username', formData.username);
      formDataToSend.append('password', formData.password);
      formDataToSend.append('num_modules', formData.num_modules);

      // Debug: Log form data
      console.log('Form data being sent:');
      for (let [key, value] of formDataToSend.entries()) {
        console.log(key, value);
      }

      // Call the login backend API
      console.log('Making API call...');
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/login`, formDataToSend, {
        headers: {
          'Content-Type': 'multipart/form-data',
        },
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      console.log('API Response:', response);
      console.log('Response data:', response.data);

      if (response.data.success) {
        console.log('Login successful, navigating...');
        navigate('/input-index', {
          state: {
            num_modules: numModules,
            username: formData.username
          }
        });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Login error:', error);
      console.error('Error response:', error.response);
      console.error('Error status:', error.response?.status);
      console.error('Error data:', error.response?.data);
      
      const errorMessage = error.response?.data?.detail || 'Login failed. Please try again.'; 
      setMessage(errorMessage);
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };
// ...existing code...
````

````python
// ...existing code...
    @app.post('/api/login')
    async def api_login(
        request: Request,
        username: str = Form(...),
        password: str = Form(...),
        num_modules: int = Form(...)
    ):
        print(f"Login attempt - Username: {username}, Password: {'*' * len(password)}, Modules: {num_modules}")
        
        if not validate_login(username, password):
            print("Validation failed")
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")
        
        print("Validation successful")
        request.session["username"] = username
        request.session["password"] = password

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}
// ...existing code...
````

GitHub Copilot: Looking at the error logs, there are two issues to address:

## Issue 1: Build Location (Washington D.C.)
This is normal - Vercel automatically chooses the deployment region based on your location and optimal performance. The location doesn't affect functionality.

## Issue 2: Main Problem - `react-scripts: command not found`

This error occurs because Vercel is trying to run the build from the **root directory** instead of the **frontend directory**. The root directory doesn't have `node_modules` or `react-scripts`.

## Solution: Configure Vercel Root Directory

### Step 1: Update Vercel Project Settings

1. **Go to your Vercel dashboard**
2. **Click on your project** (`ntu-add-drop-automator`)
3. **Go to Settings** → **General**
4. **Update these settings**:
   - **Root Directory**: frontend ✅
   - **Framework Preset**: `Create React App`
   - **Build Command**: `npm run build`
   - **Output Directory**: `build`
   - **Install Command**: `npm install`

### Step 2: Alternative - Create `vercel.json` in Root Directory

If the dashboard settings don't work, create this file in your **root directory**:

````json
{
  "buildCommand": "cd frontend && npm install && npm run build",
  "outputDirectory": "frontend/build",
  "installCommand": "cd frontend && npm install"
}
````

### Step 3: Verify Package.json Location

Make sure your `package.json` is in the correct location:

````
ntu-add-drop-automator-v2/
├── frontend/
│   ├── package.json          ← Should be here
│   ├── src/
│   │   ├── components/
│   │   └── config/
│   └── public/
└── backend/
    └── app.py
````

### Step 4: Check Your Frontend Package.json

Ensure your frontend `package.json` has the correct scripts:

````json
{
  "name": "ntu-add-drop-automator-frontend",
  "scripts": {
    "start": "react-scripts start",
    "build": "react-scripts build",
    "test": "react-scripts test",
    "eject": "react-scripts eject"
  },
  "dependencies": {
    "react": "^18.x.x",
    "react-scripts": "^5.x.x"
  }
}
````

`https://ntu-add-drop-automator-30dxt6xj3-joshua-foos-projects.vercel.app`

But your backend CORS settings only allow:

`https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app`

## Solution: Update Backend CORS Settings

Your backend needs to include the actual Vercel domain that's being used:

````python
// ...existing code...
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000", # React dev server (port 3000)
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app", # Original domain
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-30dxt6xj3-joshua-foos-projects.vercel.app", # Current domain
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-v2-backend.onrender.com",  # Backend domain
        ],
        allow_credentials=True, # Important for session cookies
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
// ...existing code...
````

## Why This Happened

Vercel creates **deployment-specific URLs** like:
- `https://ntu-add-drop-automator-30dxt6xj3-joshua-foos-projects.vercel.app` (current deployment)
- `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app` (production domain)

The deployment URL changes with each deployment, but you should have a consistent production URL.

## Better Solution: Wildcard or Production Domain

### Option 1: Use Wildcard (for development)
````python
// ...existing code...
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000",
            "https://*.vercel.app",  # Allow all Vercel subdomains
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
        ],
        allow_credentials=True,
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
// ...existing code...
````

### Option 2: Set Up Production Domain in Vercel

1. **Go to Vercel dashboard** → Your project → **Settings** → **Domains**
2. **Add a production domain** like `ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app`
3. **Set it as primary domain**

 why?

GitHub Copilot: This is happening because of how Vercel handles deployments and domains. Let me explain what's going on:

## Vercel Domain Types

Vercel creates **multiple URLs** for each project:

1. **Production Domain** (clean): `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app`
2. **Deployment-specific URLs** (long): `https://ntu-add-drop-automator-30dxt6xj3-joshua-foos-projects.vercel.app`

## Why This Happens

### The Long URL Format:
`https://[project-name]-[deployment-hash]-[username].vercel.app`

- `ntu-add-drop-automator` = your project name
- `30dxt6xj3` = unique deployment hash
- `joshua-foos-projects` = your Vercel account/team name

## Most Likely Causes:

### 1. **Branch/Environment Mismatch**
- Your homepage might be served from the **production branch**
- Your navigation might be hitting a **preview/development deployment**

### 2. **Routing Issue in Your React App**
- When you navigate using React Router, it should stay on the same domain
- If the URL changes completely, something is redirecting you

### 3. **Vercel Configuration Issue**
- Multiple deployments might be active
- Domain settings might not be properly configured

## How to Fix:

### Step 1: Check Your Vercel Domain Settings
1. **Go to Vercel Dashboard** → Your project → **Settings** → **Domains**
2. **Set Production Domain**: Make sure `ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app` is set as **Primary**
3. **Check Git Integration**: Ensure `main` branch is set as production branch

### Step 2: Verify Your React Router Navigation
Your navigation should use React Router, not window redirects:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
if (response.data.success) {
  // This should stay on the same domain
  navigate('/input-index', {
    state: {
      num_modules: numModules,
      username: formData.username
    }
  });
}
// ...existing code...
````

### Step 3: Check for Redirects in Your Code
Look for any:
- `window.location.href = ...`
- `window.open(...)`
- Hard redirects in your backend

### Step 4: Force Production Domain
Add this to your Vercel project settings:

1. **Settings** → **Environment Variables**
2. Add: `VERCEL_URL = ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app`

## Quick Debug:

**Check what's happening when you navigate:**

1. **Open Network tab** in DevTools
2. **Click "Input Indexes"** button
3. **Look for any redirects** (3xx status codes)
4. **Check if any API calls** are causing redirects

The issue is likely that you're being redirected to a preview deployment instead of staying on your production domain. React Router navigation should **never** change the domain - it should only change the path.

**Can you check:**
1. What's your current **production domain** set in Vercel?
2. Are you using `navigate()` from React Router or something else for navigation?

## The Issue: Preview vs Production Deployments

Vercel creates different types of deployments:

1. **Production**: `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app` (clean URL)
2. **Preview/Branch**: `https://ntu-add-drop-automator-30dxt6xj3-joshua-foos-projects.vercel.app` (long URL)

## Why This Happens During Navigation

Looking at your React Router setup in `App.js`, the navigation should stay on the same domain. However, the long URL suggests you might be accessing a **preview deployment** instead of the production deployment.

## How to Fix This:

### 1. Always Use the Production URL

Make sure you're accessing your site via the **production URL**:
```
https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app
```

**NOT** the preview URL:
```
https://ntu-add-drop-automator-30dxt6xj3-joshua-foos-projects.vercel.app
```

### 2. Check Your Vercel Deployment Status

In your Vercel dashboard:
1. Go to **Deployments** tab
2. Look for deployments with **"Production"** label
3. Make sure the latest deployment is marked as **Production**

### 3. Update CORS to Handle Both URLs

Since you might hit both URLs during development, update your backend CORS:

````python
// ...existing code...
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000", # React dev server (port 3000)
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app", # Production domain
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app", # Allow all preview deployments
            "https://*.vercel.app", # Wildcard for all Vercel preview deployments
        ],
        allow_credentials=True, # Important for session cookies
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
// ...existing code...
````

### 4. Force Production Domain (Optional)

Add this to your React app to redirect to production domain:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
import React, { useState, useEffect } from 'react';
import { BrowserRouter as Router, Routes, Route } from 'react-router-dom';

function App() {
  useEffect(() => {
    // Force redirect to production domain if on preview deployment
    if (process.env.NODE_ENV === 'production' && 
        window.location.hostname !== 'ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app' &&
        window.location.hostname.includes('vercel.app')) {
      window.location.href = `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app${window.location.pathname}`;
    }
  }, []);

  // ...rest of your App component
````

### 5. Test Navigation

To test if React Router is working correctly:

1. **Open production URL**: `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app`
2. **Fill out the form** and submit
3. **Check the URL** after navigation - it should stay `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app/input-index`
4. **If the URL changes** to the long version, there's a redirect happening somewhere

## Most Likely Cause:

You're probably accessing a **preview deployment URL** instead of the production URL. Preview deployments get the long URLs with deployment hashes.

**Solution**: Always bookmark and use `https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app` for testing.

### Update Your Backend Redis Configuration:

````python
// ...existing code...
def setup_redis_connection():
    """Set up and return Redis connection."""
    def get_redis():
        """Dependency Injection: Returns a Redis connection"""
        return redis.StrictRedis(
            host=os.environ.get("REDIS_HOST"),  # Remove default value
            port=int(os.environ.get("REDIS_PORT", 6379)),
            password=os.environ.get("REDIS_PASSWORD"),  # Remove default value
            decode_responses=True,
            socket_connect_timeout=5,  # Add timeout
            socket_timeout=5,
            retry_on_timeout=True
        )
    return get_redis
// ...existing code...
````

````python
// ...existing code...
def setup_redis_connection():
    """Set up and return Redis connection."""
    def get_redis():
        """Dependency Injection: Returns a Redis connection"""
        redis_host = os.environ.get("REDIS_HOST")
        redis_port = int(os.environ.get("REDIS_PORT", 6379))
        redis_password = os.environ.get("REDIS_PASSWORD")
        
        # For Render's free Redis, password might be None/empty
        redis_config = {
            "host": redis_host,
            "port": redis_port,
            "decode_responses": True,
            "socket_connect_timeout": 5,
            "socket_timeout": 5,
            "retry_on_timeout": True
        }
        
        # Only add password if it exists and is not empty
        if redis_password:
            redis_config["password"] = redis_password
            
        return redis.StrictRedis(**redis_config)
    return get_redis
// ...existing code...
````

## Step 1: Create `.gitignore` File

````
# Environment variables
````

## Step 2: Remove .env from Git History (if already pushed)

If you already pushed .env to GitHub, remove it:

```bash
# Remove .env from Git tracking (but keep it locally)

# Push to GitHub
git push origin main
```

## Step 3: Push Changes

After creating `.gitignore`:

```bash

git push origin main
```

```bash

```

josshhz11: once i have installed ruff as a linter:
C:\Users\joshua\Downloads\ntu-add-drop-automator-v2\frontend>ruff --version
ruff 0.14.4

how do i create a workflow for always ensuring and checking (with commands in the CLI) to get my code linted?

GitHub Copilot: Great question! Here's how to set up a linting workflow for your project using Ruff.

## Step 1: Create a Ruff Configuration File

Create `pyproject.toml` in your **backend directory** (for Python code):

````toml
[tool.ruff]
line-length = 100
target-version = "py311"

[tool.ruff.lint]
select = [
    "E",    # pycodestyle errors
    "W",    # pycodestyle warnings
    "F",    # Pyflakes
    "I",    # isort
    "C4",   # flake8-comprehensions
    "B",    # flake8-bugbear
]
ignore = [
    "E501",  # Line too long (we set it above)
    "W291",  # Trailing whitespace
]

[tool.ruff.lint.isort]
known-first-party = ["app"]
````

## Step 2: Create NPM/Linting Scripts

For your **frontend**, update your `package.json`:

````json
{
  "scripts": {
    "start": "react-scripts start",
    "build": "react-scripts build",
    "test": "react-scripts test",
    "eject": "react-scripts eject",
    "lint": "eslint src --ext .js,.jsx",
    "lint:fix": "eslint src --ext .js,.jsx --fix",
    "format": "prettier --write \"src/**/*.{js,jsx,json,css}\""
  },
  // ...rest of your package.json
}
````

For your **backend**, create a simple script. Add to `backend/Makefile` or use CLI commands:

## Step 3: Create Pre-commit Hook (Recommended)

Install pre-commit:

```bash
pip install pre-commit
```

Create `.pre-commit-config.yaml` in your **root directory**:

````yaml
repos:
  - repo: https://github.com/astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit
    rev: v0.14.4
    hooks:
      - id: ruff
        args: [--fix]
        stages: [commit]
      - id: ruff-format

  - repo: https://github.com/pre-commit/pre-commit-hooks
    rev: v4.5.0
    hooks:
      - id: trailing-whitespace
      - id: end-of-file-fixer
      - id: check-yaml
      - id: check-added-large-files

  - repo: https://github.com/pre-commit/mirrors-prettier
    rev: v3.1.1
    hooks:
      - id: prettier
        types_or: [javascript, jsx, json, markdown, css]
        files: frontend/
````

Setup pre-commit:

```bash
pre-commit install
```

## Step 4: CLI Commands for Linting

### Lint Backend (Python):

```bash
# Check for issues
ruff check backend/

# Fix issues automatically
ruff check backend/ --fix

# Format code
ruff format backend/
```

### Lint Frontend (JavaScript):

```bash
# Check for issues
cd frontend
npm run lint

# Fix issues automatically
npm run lint:fix

# Format with prettier
npm run format
```

## Step 5: Create GitHub Actions Workflow

Create `.github/workflows/lint.yml`:

````yaml
name: Linting

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main, develop ]

jobs:
  lint-backend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: Set up Python
        uses: actions/setup-python@v4
        with:
          python-version: '3.11'
      
      - name: Install Ruff
        run: pip install ruff
      
      - name: Lint with Ruff
        run: |
          ruff check backend/
          ruff format backend/ --check

  lint-frontend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
      
      - name: Set up Node
        uses: actions/setup-node@v3
        with:
          node-version: '18'
      
      - name: Install dependencies
        run: cd frontend && npm install
      
      - name: Lint with ESLint
        run: cd frontend && npm run lint
      
      - name: Check format with Prettier
        run: cd frontend && npx prettier --check "src/**/*.{js,jsx,json,css}"
````

## Step 6: Add to .gitignore

Already done! But make sure these are included:

````bash

.ruff_cache/
.pre-commit-framework
````


```bash
# Frontend

npx prettier --check "src/**/*.{js,jsx,json,css}"
```

## Step 8: Enforce in CI/CD

Your GitHub Actions workflow will now:
1. Run on every push to `main` or `develop`
2. Run on every pull request
3. Fail the build if linting issues are found
4. Prevent merging if linting fails

## Your Workflow:

```bash
# Before committing

cd frontend && npm run lint:fix  # Auto-fix JS issues

```

This ensures your code is always linted and formatted consistently!

````python
// ...existing code...
def create_app():
    """Create and configure the FastAPI application."""
    # Initialize components first
    initialize_components()

    # Initialize FastAPI app
    app = FastAPI()

    # Generate a random secret key for session encryption
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get("SECRET_KEY", secrets.token_hex(32))

        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[

            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app",
            "https://*.vercel.app",
        ],
        allow_credentials=True,
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )

    # ========================================================================
    # ROUTE DEFINITIONS
    # ========================================================================

    @app.get('/')
    async def root():
        return {"message": "NTU Add-Drop Automator Backend API", "status": "running"}

    # Add this route for health check
    @app.get('/api/health')
    async def health_check(redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            redis_db.ping()

                "status": "healthy",
                "redis": "connected",
                "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat()
            }
        except Exception as e:
            return {
                "status": "unhealthy",
                "redis": f"disconnected: {str(e)}",
                "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat()
            }
````

## Fix 3: Update Redis Connection Code

Make the Redis connection more robust:

````python
// ...existing code...
def setup_redis_connection():
    """Set up and return Redis connection."""
    def get_redis():
        """Dependency Injection: Returns a Redis connection"""
        redis_host = os.environ.get("REDIS_HOST")
        redis_port = int(os.environ.get("REDIS_PORT", 6379))
        redis_password = os.environ.get("REDIS_PASSWORD")
        
        if not redis_host:
            raise ValueError("REDIS_HOST environment variable is not set")

        redis_config = {
            "host": redis_host,
            "port": redis_port,
            "decode_responses": True,
            "socket_connect_timeout": 10,  # Increased timeout
            "socket_timeout": 10,
            "retry_on_timeout": True
        }

        # Only add password if it exists and is not empty
        if redis_password and redis_password.strip():
            redis_config["password"] = redis_password
        
        try:
            return redis.StrictRedis(**redis_config)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Redis connection error: {str(e)}")
            raise
    return get_redis
// ...existing code...
````

do you know why? for some reason it is validating in the previous form submission on /api/login, and it is supposed to store the credentials in the session. is it because in dev tools i had disable cache ticked?

also are these APIs written accurately?

# API route to process first form submission page on the frontend (username, password, numModules)
    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(
        request: Request,
        username: str = Form(...),
        password: str = Form(...),
        num_modules: int = Form(...),
    ):
        if not validate_login(username, password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Store credentials in session
        request.session["username"] = username
        request.session["password"] = password

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    # API route to process module indexes (second form submission page on the frontend - [old_index, new_indexes] for each module)
    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            # Get credentials stored in the session
            username = request.session.get("username")
            password = request.session.get("password")

            # Should I just call this once? Maybe just here? and no need to do this in api_login (save on backend API calls - although we are still calling the backend, just not performing the login)
            if not validate_login(username, password):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Not authenticated")

            num_modules = form_data.get("num_modules", 0)
            module_data = form_data.get("modules", [])

            if num_modules <= 0 or len(module_data) != num_modules:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid module data")

            # Parse modules into swap_items
            swap_items = []  # List to store (old_index, new_indexes, swapped)
            for i, module in enumerate(module_data):
                old_index = module.get("old_index")
                new_indexes_raw = module.get("new_indexes")

                if not old_index or not new_indexes_raw:
                    raise HTTPException(
                        status_code=400,
                        detail=f"Missing or invalid data for module {i + 1}",
                    )

                new_index_list = [
                    index.strip()
                    for index in new_indexes_raw.split(",")
                    if index.strip()
                ]

                swap_items.append(
                    {
                        "old_index": old_index,
                        "new_indexes": new_index_list,
                        "swapped": False,
                    }
                )

            # Generate a unique swap id for this swap session
            swap_id = f"username_{int(time.time())}"

            # Initialize Redis with status data
            status_data = {
                "status": "Processing",
                "details": [
                    {
                        "old_index": item["old_index"],
                        "new_indexes": ", ".join(item["new_indexes"]),
                        "swapped": False,
                        "message": "Pending...",
                    }
                    for item in swap_items
                ],
                "message": "Your swap request is being processed.",  # Was previously None
            }
            redis_db.set(swap_id, json.dumps(status_data))

            # Start background thread for to execute the swap operation
            thread = threading.Thread(
                target=perform_swaps,
                args=(username, password, swap_items, swap_id, redis_db),
                daemon=True,
            )
            thread.start()

            return {
                "success": True,
                "swap_id": swap_id,
                "message": "Swap process started successfully",
            }

        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=500, detail=f"Swap initiation failed: {str(e)}"
            )

i have also attached the frontend components for your reference

GitHub Copilot: Looking at your error, the issue is with **session management**. The session data isn't being preserved between the `/api/login` and `/api/submit-swap` calls. Here's what's happening and how to fix it:

## The Problem

1. **Sessions require cookies** to work properly
2. Your frontend isn't handling session cookies correctly
3. The `validate_login()` function only checks if username/password exist, but they're `None` because the session isn't preserved

## Fix 1: Update Frontend to Handle Sessions Properly

Your frontend is sending credentials as `FormData` in login but as `JSON` in submit-swap. This inconsistency can cause session issues.

### Update HomePage.js:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  const handleSubmit = async (event) => {
    event.preventDefault();
    setLoading(true);
    setMessage('');

    try {
      // Validate that num_modules is a positive number
      const numModules = parseInt(formData.num_modules);
      if (numModules < 1 || numModules > 6) {
        setMessage('Please enter a valid number of modules (1-6)');
        setLoading(false);
        return;
      }
      
      // Send as JSON instead of FormData for consistency
      const loginData = {
        username: formData.username,
        password: formData.password,
        num_modules: numModules
      };

      // Call the login backend API
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/login`, loginData, {
        headers: {
          'Content-Type': 'application/json', // Changed from multipart/form-data
        },
        withCredentials: true, // Important for session cookies
      });

      if (response.data.success) {
        // Navigate to the input-index page on the frontend
        navigate('/input-index', {
          state: {
            num_modules: numModules,
            username: formData.username
          }
        });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Login error:', error);
      const errorMessage = error.response?.data?.detail || 'Login failed. Please try again.'; 
      setMessage(errorMessage);
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };
// ...existing code...
````

## Fix 2: Update Backend API to Accept JSON

Change your `/api/login` endpoint to accept JSON instead of form data:

````python
// ...existing code...
from pydantic import BaseModel

# Add this model for login request
class LoginRequest(BaseModel):
    username: str
    password: str
    num_modules: int

# ...existing code...

    # API route to process first form submission page on the frontend (username, password, numModules)
    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest):
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Store credentials in session
        request.session["username"] = login_data.username
        request.session["password"] = login_data.password

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}
// ...existing code...
````

````python
    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")

        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            # Debug session data
            print(f"Session data: {dict(request.session)}")
            
            # Get credentials stored in the session

            password = request.session.get("password")
            
            # Debug credentials

            print(f"Password from session: {'*' * len(password) if password else None}")

            if not validate_login(username, password):
                print("Session validation failed - credentials not found in session")
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Not authenticated")

            # ...rest of your code...
````

## Fix 4: Check Session Middleware Configuration

Make sure your session middleware is properly configured with CORS:

````python
// ...existing code...
def create_app():
    """Create and configure the FastAPI application."""
    # Initialize components first
    initialize_components()

    # Initialize FastAPI app
    app = FastAPI()

    # Generate a random secret key for session encryption
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get("SECRET_KEY", secrets.token_hex(32))
    app.add_middleware(
        SessionMiddleware, 
        secret_key=SECRET_KEY,
        same_site="none",  # Required for cross-origin requests
        https_only=False   # Set to True in production with HTTPS
    )

    # CORS configuration (must come AFTER SessionMiddleware)
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app",
            "https://*.vercel.app",
        ],
        allow_credentials=True,  # Essential for sessions
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
// ...existing code...
````

## Fix 5: Alternative Approach - Store Data in Frontend

If sessions continue to be problematic, you can store the credentials temporarily in the frontend and pass them with each request:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  const handleSubmit = async (e) => {
    e.preventDefault();
    setLoading(true);
    setMessage({ type: '', text: '' });

    try {
      // Get username from navigation state
      const username = location.state?.username;
      
      if (!username) {
        throw new Error('Session expired. Please log in again.');
      }

      const modules = [];
      for (let i = 0; i < numModules; i++) {
        const oldIndex = formData[`old_index_${i}`]
        const newIndexes = formData[`new_index_${i}`]

        if (!oldIndex || !newIndexes) {
          throw new Error(`Please fill in all fields for Module ${i + 1}`);
        }
        
        modules.push({
          old_index: oldIndex,
          new_indexes: newIndexes
        });
      }

      // Include username in the request (temporary solution)
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/submit-swap`, {
        num_modules: numModules,
        modules: modules,
        username: username  // Add this temporarily
      }, {
        headers: {
          'Content-Type': 'application/json',
        },
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      if (response.data.success) {
        navigate('/swap-status', {
          state: {
            swap_id: response.data.swap_id,
            numModules,
            formData
          }
        });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Swap submission error:', error);
      const errorMessage = error.response?.data?.detail || error.message || 'Swap submission failed. Please try again.';
      setMessage({ type: 'error', text: errorMessage });
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };
// ...existing code...
````

GitHub Copilot: Looking at the error details, I can see the exact issue! The error message shows:

```
"detail": "Swap initiation failed: 401: Not authenticated"
```

This means the session authentication is failing. Let me analyze what's happening:

## The Problem

1. **Login works** (you get to the next page)
2. **Session data gets lost** between `/api/login` and `/api/submit-swap`
3. The `validate_login(username, password)` fails because `username` and `password` are `None`

## Root Cause: Session Middleware Configuration

Your session middleware needs specific settings for cross-origin requests. Update your backend:

````python
// ...existing code...
def create_app():
    """Create and configure the FastAPI application."""
    # Initialize components first
    initialize_components()

    # Initialize FastAPI app
    app = FastAPI()

    # Generate a random secret key for session encryption
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get("SECRET_KEY", secrets.token_hex(32))
    
    # Fix session middleware for cross-origin
    app.add_middleware(
        SessionMiddleware, 
        secret_key=SECRET_KEY,
        same_site="none",     # Required for cross-origin
        https_only=True,      # Required for same_site="none"
        max_age=1800         # 30 minutes session timeout
    )

    # CORS configuration (must come AFTER SessionMiddleware)
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app",
            "https://*.vercel.app",
        ],
        allow_credentials=True,  # Essential for sessions
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )
// ...existing code...
````

````python
                )
````

## Quick Fix Option: Store Credentials in Frontend State

As a temporary workaround, you can store credentials in the frontend and pass them with the request:

### Update InputIndex.js:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  const handleSubmit = async (e) => {
    e.preventDefault();
    setLoading(true);
    setMessage({ type: '', text: '' });

    try {
      // Get username from navigation state (passed from login)
      const username = location.state?.username;
      
      if (!username) {
        throw new Error('Session expired. Please log in again.');
      }

      const modules = [];
      for (let i = 0; i < numModules; i++) {
        const oldIndex = formData[`old_index_${i}`]
        const newIndexes = formData[`new_index_${i}`]

        if (!oldIndex || !newIndexes) {
          throw new Error(`Please fill in all fields for Module ${i + 1}`);
        }
        
        modules.push({
          old_index: oldIndex,
          new_indexes: newIndexes
        });
      }

      // Include username in the request data
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/submit-swap`, {
        num_modules: numModules,
        modules: modules,
        username: username  // Add this as backup
      }, {
        headers: {
          'Content-Type': 'application/json',
        },
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      if (response.data.success) {
        navigate('/swap-status', {
          state: {
            swap_id: response.data.swap_id,
            numModules,
            formData
          }
        });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Swap submission error:', error);
      
      // If authentication error, redirect to login
      if (error.response?.status === 401) {
        setMessage({ 
          type: 'error', 
          text: 'Session expired. Redirecting to login...' 
        });
        setTimeout(() => navigate('/'), 2000);
        return;
      }
      
      const errorMessage = error.response?.data?.detail || error.message || 'Swap submission failed. Please try again.';
      setMessage({ type: 'error', text: errorMessage });
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };
// ...existing code...
````

## Current Data Storage Analysis

### 1. **Session Storage (FastAPI Session)**
Currently stores:
- `username` (plaintext) ❌
- `password` (plaintext) ❌

**Problems:**

## Security Issues & Fixes

### Problem 1: Plaintext Password Storage

**Current Issue:**
````python
# BAD - Storing plaintext password
request.session["username"] = login_data.username
request.session["password"] = login_data.password  # ❌ Security risk
````

**Fix Option 1: Don't Store Passwords**
````python
// ...existing code...
    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest):
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Only store username and validated flag
        request.session["username"] = login_data.username
        request.session["authenticated"] = True
        request.session["login_time"] = time.time()
        # DON'T store password

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            # Check authentication status instead of password
            username = request.session.get("username")
            authenticated = request.session.get("authenticated")
            login_time = request.session.get("login_time")

            if not username or not authenticated:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Not authenticated")
            
            # Check session timeout (30 minutes for security)
            if time.time() - login_time > 1800:  # 30 minutes
                request.session.clear()
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired")

            # Get password from request data (user re-enters for security)
            password = form_data.get("password")
            if not password:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Password required for swap execution")

            # Validate credentials again
            if not validate_login(username, password):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credentials")

            # ...rest of your swap logic...
// ...existing code...
````

**Fix Option 2: Hash Password (Better)**
````python
import hashlib
import secrets

// ...existing code...
def hash_password(password: str, salt: str = None) -> tuple:
    """Hash password with salt"""
    if salt is None:
        salt = secrets.token_hex(16)
    
    # Use a strong hashing algorithm
    password_hash = hashlib.pbkdf2_hmac('sha256', 
                                       password.encode('utf-8'), 
                                       salt.encode('utf-8'), 
                                       100000)  # 100k iterations
    return password_hash.hex(), salt

def verify_password(password: str, password_hash: str, salt: str) -> bool:
    """Verify password against hash"""
    hash_to_check, _ = hash_password(password, salt)
    return hash_to_check == password_hash

    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest):
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Hash password before storing
        password_hash, salt = hash_password(login_data.password)
        
        request.session["username"] = login_data.username
        request.session["password_hash"] = password_hash
        request.session["salt"] = salt
        request.session["authenticated"] = True
        request.session["login_time"] = time.time()

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            username = request.session.get("username")
            password_hash = request.session.get("password_hash")
            salt = request.session.get("salt")
            
            if not all([username, password_hash, salt]):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Not authenticated")

            # For swap execution, we still need the actual password
            # Option: Ask user to re-enter password for security
            password = form_data.get("password")  # Add this to frontend
            if not verify_password(password, password_hash, salt):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credentials")

            # ...rest of your swap logic...
// ...existing code...
````

## Data Flow Visualization

### Current Flow:
```
1. Login Form → FastAPI Session: {username, password}
2. Input Indexes → Keep session alive
3. Submit Swap → Redis: {swap_id: status_data} + Background thread
4. Swap Status → Poll Redis for updates
```

### Recommended Secure Flow:
```
1. Login Form → FastAPI Session: {username, password_hash, salt, authenticated}
2. Input Indexes → Validate session timeout
3. Submit Swap → User re-enters password → Verify → Start swap
4. Swap Status → Poll Redis (no sensitive data)
```

## Timing Functionality Check

### Current Implementation Analysis:

````python
// Your current timing logic in perform_swaps():
def perform_swaps(username, password, swap_items, swap_id, redis_db):
    # ...setup...
    
    start_time = time.time()  # ✅ Start timer
    while True:
        # Attempt swaps for all modules
        for idx, item in enumerate(swap_items):
            if not item["swapped"]:
                # Try each new_index until one succeeds
                for new_index in item["new_indexes"]:
                    success, message = attempt_swap(...)
                    if success:
                        item["swapped"] = True
                        break
                        
        # Check if all done
        all_swapped = all(item["swapped"] for item in swap_items)
        if all_swapped:
            update_overall_status(redis_db, swap_id, status="Completed", ...)
            break

        # Check 2-hour timeout
        if time.time() - start_time >= 2 * 3600:  # ✅ 2 hours = 7200 seconds
            update_overall_status(redis_db, swap_id, status="Timed Out", ...)
            break

        time.sleep(5 * 60)  # ✅ Wait 5 minutes = 300 seconds
````

### Issues Found:

1. **✅ 2-hour timeout: Working correctly**
2. **✅ 5-minute intervals: Working correctly**
3. **❌ Session timeout not enforced in background thread**

### Fix Session Management:

````python
// ...existing code...
def perform_swaps(username, password, swap_items, swap_id, redis_db):
    driver = None
    swap_start_time = time.time()

    try:
        driver = get_driver()
        
        # Initial login
        login_success = login_to_portal(driver, username, password, swap_id, redis_db)
        if not login_success:
            return

        while True:
            # Check if we've exceeded 2 hours
            elapsed_time = time.time() - swap_start_time
            if elapsed_time >= 2 * 3600:  # 2 hours
                update_overall_status(
                    redis_db,
                    swap_id,
                    status="Timed Out",
                    message=f"Time limit reached after {elapsed_time/3600:.1f} hours."
                )
                break

            # Attempt swaps for all unswapped modules
            swap_attempted_this_cycle = False
            for idx, item in enumerate(swap_items):
                if not item["swapped"]:
                    swap_attempted_this_cycle = True
                    failed_indexes = []
                    
                    for new_index in item["new_indexes"]:
                        try:
                            success, message = attempt_swap(
                                old_index=item["old_index"],
                                new_index=new_index,
                                idx=idx,
                                driver=driver,
                                swap_id=swap_id,
                                redis_db=redis_db,
                            )
                            
                            if success:
                                item["swapped"] = True
                                update_status(
                                    redis_db,
                                    swap_id,
                                    idx,
                                    f"Successfully swapped {item['old_index']} → {new_index}",
                                    success=True,
                                )
                                break
                            else:
                                failed_indexes.append(new_index)
                                
                        except WebDriverException as e:
                            print(f"WebDriver error: {e}")
                            # Re-login if driver fails
                            release_driver(driver)
                            driver = get_driver()
                            login_to_portal(driver, username, password, swap_id, redis_db)
                            failed_indexes.append(new_index)
                            
                    # Update status for failed attempts
                    if not item["swapped"] and failed_indexes:
                        update_status(
                            redis_db,
                            swap_id,
                            idx,
                            f"Indexes {', '.join(failed_indexes)} have no vacancies. Will retry in 5 minutes.",
                        )

            # Check if all modules are swapped
            all_swapped = all(item["swapped"] for item in swap_items)
            if all_swapped:
                update_overall_status(
                    redis_db,
                    swap_id,
                    status="Completed",
                    message="All modules have been successfully swapped!"
                )
                break

            # Only sleep if we attempted swaps (avoid infinite loop if no modules)
            if swap_attempted_this_cycle:
                remaining_time = (2 * 3600) - elapsed_time
                next_attempt_in = min(5 * 60, remaining_time)  # 5 minutes or remaining time
                
                if next_attempt_in > 0:
                    update_overall_status(
                        redis_db,
                        swap_id,
                        status="Processing",
                        message=f"Next attempt in {int(next_attempt_in/60)} minutes. Time remaining: {int(remaining_time/60)} minutes."
                    )
                    time.sleep(next_attempt_in)
                else:
                    break
            else:
                # No modules to swap (shouldn't happen, but safety check)
                update_overall_status(
                    redis_db,
                    swap_id,
                    status="Error",
                    message="No modules found to swap."
                )
                break

    except Exception as e:
        update_overall_status(
            redis_db, 
            swap_id, 
            status="Error", 
            message=f"An error occurred: {str(e)}"
        )
    finally:
        if driver:
            release_driver(driver)
// ...existing code...
````

2. **Add session timeouts**
3. **Require password re-entry for sensitive operations**
4. **Use HTTPS-only cookies**
5. **Add CSRF protection**

for the password hashing, for the swap attempts every 5 minutes, we still need the users passwords, meaning once we hash them, we still need to be able to unhash them, since its not our website that they are logging into, where we can just compare their hashed passwords with the hashed passwords in the database. hence, we need to store a hash version of the passwords, whether it's in the session, or in the backend as well, or not.

what is the best way for us to do this optimally?

GitHub Copilot: You're absolutely right! This is a critical security dilemma. Since you need to **actually log into NTU's external system** every 5 minutes for 2 hours, you need the **plaintext password** to authenticate with their servers. Hashing won't work because:

1. **You can't "unhash" passwords** - hashing is one-way
2. **NTU's system needs the actual password** - not a hash
3. **You need it repeatedly** - every 5 minutes for 2 hours

Here are the **optimal security approaches**:

## Option 1: Encrypted Storage (Recommended)

Store passwords **encrypted** (not hashed) so you can decrypt them when needed:

````python
import hashlib
import secrets
from cryptography.fernet import Fernet
import base64

// ...existing code...

def get_encryption_key():
    """Generate or get encryption key from environment"""
    key = os.environ.get("ENCRYPTION_KEY")
    if not key:
        # Generate a new key (do this once and store in env vars)
        key = Fernet.generate_key().decode()
        print(f"Generated new encryption key: {key}")
        print("Add this to your environment variables: ENCRYPTION_KEY={key}")
    return key.encode()

def encrypt_password(password: str) -> str:
    """Encrypt password for secure storage"""
    key = get_encryption_key()
    f = Fernet(key)
    encrypted_password = f.encrypt(password.encode())
    return base64.urlsafe_b64encode(encrypted_password).decode()

def decrypt_password(encrypted_password: str) -> str:
    """Decrypt password for use"""
    key = get_encryption_key()
    f = Fernet(key)
    encrypted_bytes = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(encrypted_password.encode())
    decrypted_password = f.decrypt(encrypted_bytes)
    return decrypted_password.decode()

    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest):
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Encrypt password before storing in session
        encrypted_password = encrypt_password(login_data.password)
        
        request.session["username"] = login_data.username
        request.session["encrypted_password"] = encrypted_password
        request.session["authenticated"] = True
        request.session["login_time"] = time.time()

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            username = request.session.get("username")
            encrypted_password = request.session.get("encrypted_password")
            authenticated = request.session.get("authenticated")
            login_time = request.session.get("login_time")
            
            if not all([username, encrypted_password, authenticated]):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Not authenticated")

            # Check session timeout (30 minutes for security)
            if time.time() - login_time > 1800:  # 30 minutes
                request.session.clear()
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired")

            # Decrypt password for use
            password = decrypt_password(encrypted_password)

            # Validate credentials again (optional security check)
            if not validate_login(username, password):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credentials")

            # ...rest of your swap logic...
            # Pass the decrypted password to perform_swaps
            thread = threading.Thread(
                target=perform_swaps,
                args=(username, password, swap_items, swap_id, redis_db),
                daemon=True,
            )
            thread.start()

            return {
                "success": True,
                "swap_id": swap_id,
                "message": "Swap process started successfully",
            }

        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=500, detail=f"Swap initiation failed: {str(e)}"
            )
// ...existing code...
````

## Option 2: Store in Redis with TTL (Alternative)

Store encrypted credentials in Redis with automatic expiration:

````python
// ...existing code...
    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Create a session ID
        session_id = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
        
        # Encrypt and store credentials in Redis with 2-hour TTL
        encrypted_password = encrypt_password(login_data.password)
        session_data = {
            "username": login_data.username,
            "encrypted_password": encrypted_password,
            "authenticated": True,
            "login_time": time.time()
        }
        
        # Store in Redis with 2-hour expiration (7200 seconds)
        redis_db.setex(f"session:{session_id}", 7200, json.dumps(session_data))
        
        # Store session ID in FastAPI session
        request.session["session_id"] = session_id

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            session_id = request.session.get("session_id")
            if not session_id:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="No session found")

            # Get credentials from Redis
            session_data_json = redis_db.get(f"session:{session_id}")
            if not session_data_json:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired")

            session_data = json.loads(session_data_json)
            username = session_data["username"]
            encrypted_password = session_data["encrypted_password"]
            
            # Decrypt password
            password = decrypt_password(encrypted_password)

            # ...rest of your swap logic...
            
        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=500, detail=f"Swap initiation failed: {str(e)}"
            )
// ...existing code...
````
