Store passwords encrypted (not hashed) so you can decrypt them when needed:
import hashlib
import secrets
from cryptography.fernet import Fernet
import base64

// ...existing code...

def get_encryption_key():
    """Generate or get encryption key from environment"""
    key = os.environ.get("ENCRYPTION_KEY")
    if not key:
        # Generate a new key (do this once and store in env vars)
        key = Fernet.generate_key().decode()
        print(f"Generated new encryption key: {key}")
        print("Add this to your environment variables: ENCRYPTION_KEY={key}")
    return key.encode()

def encrypt_password(password: str) -> str:
    """Encrypt password for secure storage"""
    key = get_encryption_key()
    f = Fernet(key)
    encrypted_password = f.encrypt(password.encode())
    return base64.urlsafe_b64encode(encrypted_password).decode()

def decrypt_password(encrypted_password: str) -> str:
    """Decrypt password for use"""
    key = get_encryption_key()
    f = Fernet(key)
    encrypted_bytes = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(encrypted_password.encode())
    decrypted_password = f.decrypt(encrypted_bytes)
    return decrypted_password.decode()

    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest):

        encrypted_password = encrypt_password(login_data.password)

            if not all([username, encrypted_password, authenticated]):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Not authenticated")

            # Check session timeout (30 minutes for security)
            if time.time() - login_time > 1800:  # 30 minutes
                request.session.clear()
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired")

            # Decrypt password for use
            password = decrypt_password(encrypted_password)

            # Validate credentials again (optional security check)
            if not validate_login(username, password):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credentials")

GitHub Copilot: Excellent questions! You're absolutely right about security concerns with open-sourcing and code organization. Let me address both:

## 1. Storage Location: Redis vs Session

**Recommendation: Store in Redis with TTL** (more secure for open-source)

### Why Redis is Better:

**Session Storage Issues:**
- ❌ Session data visible in server memory dumps
- ❌ Session cookies can be inspected (even if encrypted)
- ❌ Harder to manage cleanup across server restarts

**Redis Storage Benefits:**
- ✅ Data isolated from HTTP layer
- ✅ Automatic expiration with TTL
- ✅ Better access control
- ✅ Easier to audit and monitor

## 2. Complete Implementation

Here's a clean, organized implementation:

````python
import json
import threading
import redis
import uvicorn
from fastapi import FastAPI, Depends, HTTPException, Form, Request
from pydantic import BaseModel
from selenium import webdriver
# ... your existing imports ...
from cryptography.fernet import Fernet
import base64

# ============================================================================
# SECURITY AND ENCRYPTION FUNCTIONS
# ============================================================================

def get_encryption_key():
    """Get encryption key from environment variables"""
    key = os.environ.get("ENCRYPTION_KEY")
    if not key:
        raise ValueError("ENCRYPTION_KEY environment variable is required")
    return key.encode()

def encrypt_password(password: str) -> str:
    """Encrypt password for secure storage"""
    key = get_encryption_key()
    f = Fernet(key)
    encrypted_password = f.encrypt(password.encode())
    return base64.urlsafe_b64encode(encrypted_password).decode()

def decrypt_password(encrypted_password: str) -> str:
    """Decrypt password for use"""
    key = get_encryption_key()
    f = Fernet(key)
    encrypted_bytes = base64.urlsafe_b64decode(encrypted_password.encode())
    decrypted_password = f.decrypt(encrypted_bytes)
    return decrypted_password.decode()

def create_secure_session(redis_db, username: str, password: str) -> str:
    """Create secure session with encrypted credentials in Redis"""
    session_id = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
    
    # Encrypt password before storing
    encrypted_password = encrypt_password(password)
    
    # Store in Redis with 2-hour TTL (7200 seconds)
    session_data = {
        "username": username,
        "encrypted_password": encrypted_password,
        "authenticated": True,
        "created_at": time.time(),
        "expires_at": time.time() + 7200  # 2 hours
    }
    
    redis_db.setex(f"session:{session_id}", 7200, json.dumps(session_data))
    return session_id

def get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id: str) -> dict:
    """Retrieve and validate secure session"""
    if not session_id:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="No session ID provided")
    
    session_data_json = redis_db.get(f"session:{session_id}")
    if not session_data_json:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired or invalid")
    
    session_data = json.loads(session_data_json)
    
    # Additional expiration check
    if time.time() > session_data.get("expires_at", 0):
        redis_db.delete(f"session:{session_id}")
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired")
    
    return session_data

def get_decrypted_credentials(redis_db, session_id: str) -> tuple:
    """Get decrypted username and password from secure session"""
    session_data = get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id)
    
    username = session_data["username"]
    encrypted_password = session_data["encrypted_password"]
    password = decrypt_password(encrypted_password)
    
    return username, password

# ============================================================================
# YOUR EXISTING UTILITY FUNCTIONS
# ============================================================================

# ... keep all your existing functions (check_chrome_paths, setup_redis_connection, etc.)

# ============================================================================
# APPLICATION SETUP
# ============================================================================

def create_app():
    """Create and configure the FastAPI application."""
    # Initialize components first
    initialize_components()

    # Initialize FastAPI app
    app = FastAPI()

    # Simpler session middleware (only for session ID)
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get("SECRET_KEY", secrets.token_hex(32))
    app.add_middleware(
        SessionMiddleware,
        secret_key=SECRET_KEY,
        same_site="none",
        https_only=True,
        max_age=7200
    )

    # CORS configuration
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app",
            "https://*.vercel.app",
        ],
        allow_credentials=True,
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )

    # ========================================================================
    # ROUTE DEFINITIONS
    # ========================================================================

    @app.get("/")
    async def root():
        return {"message": "NTU Add-Drop Automator Backend API", "status": "running"}

    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        # Validate credentials
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Create secure session in Redis
        session_id = create_secure_session(redis_db, login_data.username, login_data.password)
        
        # Store only session ID in FastAPI session
        request.session["session_id"] = session_id
        request.session["authenticated"] = True

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            # Get session ID from FastAPI session
            session_id = request.session.get("session_id")
            if not session_id:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="No active session")

            # Get decrypted credentials from Redis
            username, password = get_decrypted_credentials(redis_db, session_id)

            # Validate credentials again for security
            if not validate_login(username, password):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credentials")

            # Parse module data
            num_modules = form_data.get("num_modules", 0)
            module_data = form_data.get("modules", [])

            if num_modules <= 0 or len(module_data) != num_modules:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid module data")

            # Parse modules into swap_items
            swap_items = []
            for i, module in enumerate(module_data):
                old_index = module.get("old_index")
                new_indexes_raw = module.get("new_indexes")

                if not old_index or not new_indexes_raw:
                    raise HTTPException(
                        status_code=400,
                        detail=f"Missing or invalid data for module {i + 1}",
                    )

                new_index_list = [
                    index.strip()
                    for index in new_indexes_raw.split(",")
                    if index.strip()
                ]

                swap_items.append({
                    "old_index": old_index,
                    "new_indexes": new_index_list,
                    "swapped": False,
                })

            # Generate unique swap ID
            swap_id = f"{username}_{int(time.time())}"

            # Initialize Redis with status data
            status_data = {
                "status": "Processing",
                "details": [
                    {
                        "old_index": item["old_index"],
                        "new_indexes": ", ".join(item["new_indexes"]),
                        "swapped": False,
                        "message": "Pending...",
                    }
                    for item in swap_items
                ],
                "message": "Your swap request is being processed.",
            }
            redis_db.set(swap_id, json.dumps(status_data))

            # Start background thread
            thread = threading.Thread(
                target=perform_swaps,
                args=(username, password, swap_items, swap_id, redis_db),
                daemon=True,
            )
            thread.start()

            return {
                "success": True,
                "swap_id": swap_id,
                "message": "Swap process started successfully",
            }

        except HTTPException:
            raise  # Re-raise HTTP exceptions as-is
        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=500, detail=f"Swap initiation failed: {str(e)}"
            )

    @app.get("/api/swap-status/{swap_id}")
    async def api_get_swap_status(swap_id: str, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        status_data = get_status_data(redis_db, swap_id)
        return status_data

    @app.post("/api/stop-swap/{swap_id}")
    async def api_stop_swap(swap_id: str, request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        # Update Redis to stop the process
        status_data = get_status_data(redis_db, swap_id)
        status_data["status"] = "Stopped"
        set_status_data(redis_db, swap_id, status_data)

        # Clean up session
        session_id = request.session.get("session_id")
        if session_id:
            redis_db.delete(f"session:{session_id}")
        request.session.clear()
        redis_db.delete(swap_id)

        return {"success": True, "message": "Swap successfully stopped"}

    @app.get("/api/health")
    async def health_check(redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            redis_db.ping()
            return {
                "status": "healthy",
                "redis": "connected",
                "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            }
        except Exception as e:
            return {
                "status": "unhealthy",
                "redis": f"disconnected: {str(e)}",
                "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            }

    return app

# ============================================================================
# MAIN FUNCTION
# ============================================================================

# Initialize app at the module level
app = create_app()

# ... rest of your existing code ...
````

## 5. Security Benefits

## 6. Data Flow

```
1. Login → Create session in Redis with encrypted password
2. Store session_id in HTTP session
3. Submit-swap → Get session_id → Decrypt credentials from Redis
4. Pass decrypted password to Selenium for NTU login
5. Auto-cleanup after 2 hours
```

This approach keeps your code organized and secure for open-source distribution!

GitHub Copilot: ## 1. Fernet Encryption Keys

Yes, the `=` at the end is **completely correct**! Fernet keys are base64-encoded, and base64 encoding uses `=` for padding. This is normal and expected.

Your keys are valid:
- `fE-vSqdUZZK-xWk4vDqmx9jwKfOqABllngKzijfKZuU=` ✅
- `jB1d7tpu_fYw-5ow7vLx5m4h1fsvlb2QLuZU-K2XiQk=` ✅

## 2. Redis Structure Optimization

You're absolutely right! **Combining into one Redis instance is much better**. Using `session_id` is also the better choice because:

✅ **Session ID is randomly generated** (more secure)  
✅ **No username exposure** in Redis keys  
✅ **Cleaner architecture** with single source of truth  
✅ **Easier cleanup** and management  

Here's the optimal combined structure:

## Recommended Combined Structure:

````python
// ...existing imports...

# ============================================================================
# ENHANCED SECURITY AND SESSION FUNCTIONS
# ============================================================================

def create_secure_session(redis_db, username: str, password: str) -> str:
    """Create secure session with encrypted credentials and swap data in Redis"""
    session_id = secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
    
    # Encrypt password before storing
    encrypted_password = encrypt_password(password)
    
    # Combined session and swap data structure
    session_data = {
        # Session info
        "username": username,
        "encrypted_password": encrypted_password,
        "authenticated": True,
        "created_at": time.time(),
        "expires_at": time.time() + 7200,  # 2 hours
        
        # Swap status info (initially empty)
        "swap_status": "idle",  # idle, processing, completed, error, stopped, timed_out
        "swap_message": None,
        "swap_started_at": None,
        
        # Module data (initially empty, populated when swap starts)
        "modules": []
    }
    
    # Store in Redis with 2-hour TTL
    redis_db.setex(f"session:{session_id}", 7200, json.dumps(session_data))
    return session_id

def get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id: str) -> dict:
    """Retrieve and validate secure session"""
    if not session_id:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="No session ID provided")
    
    session_data_json = redis_db.get(f"session:{session_id}")
    if not session_data_json:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired or invalid")
    
    session_data = json.loads(session_data_json)
    
    # Check expiration
    if time.time() > session_data.get("expires_at", 0):
        redis_db.delete(f"session:{session_id}")
        raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Session expired")
    
    return session_data

def update_session_data(redis_db, session_id: str, updates: dict):
    """Update session data in Redis"""
    session_data = get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id)
    session_data.update(updates)
    
    # Maintain TTL
    ttl = redis_db.ttl(f"session:{session_id}")
    if ttl > 0:
        redis_db.setex(f"session:{session_id}", ttl, json.dumps(session_data))

def get_decrypted_credentials(redis_db, session_id: str) -> tuple:
    """Get decrypted username and password from secure session"""
    session_data = get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id)
    
    username = session_data["username"]
    encrypted_password = session_data["encrypted_password"]
    password = decrypt_password(encrypted_password)
    
    return username, password

# ============================================================================
# UPDATED REDIS AND STATUS FUNCTIONS
# ============================================================================

def initialize_swap_in_session(redis_db, session_id: str, swap_items: list):
    """Initialize swap data in the existing session"""
    updates = {
        "swap_status": "processing",
        "swap_message": "Your swap request is being processed.",
        "swap_started_at": time.time(),
        "modules": [
            {
                "old_index": item["old_index"],
                "new_indexes": item["new_indexes"],
                "swapped": False,
                "message": "Pending...",
            }
            for item in swap_items
        ]
    }
    update_session_data(redis_db, session_id, updates)

def update_module_status(redis_db, session_id: str, module_idx: int, message: str, success: bool = False):
    """Update status of specific module in session"""
    session_data = get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id)
    
    if module_idx < len(session_data["modules"]):
        session_data["modules"][module_idx]["message"] = message
        if success:
            session_data["modules"][module_idx]["swapped"] = True
        
        # Update in Redis
        ttl = redis_db.ttl(f"session:{session_id}")
        if ttl > 0:
            redis_db.setex(f"session:{session_id}", ttl, json.dumps(session_data))

def update_overall_swap_status(redis_db, session_id: str, status: str, message: str):
    """Update overall swap status in session"""
    updates = {
        "swap_status": status,
        "swap_message": message
    }
    update_session_data(redis_db, session_id, updates)

# ============================================================================
# UPDATED APPLICATION ROUTES
# ============================================================================

def create_app():
    """Create and configure the FastAPI application."""
    # Initialize components first
    initialize_components()

    # Initialize FastAPI app
    app = FastAPI()

    # Session middleware configuration
    SECRET_KEY = os.environ.get("SECRET_KEY", secrets.token_hex(32))
    app.add_middleware(
        SessionMiddleware,
        secret_key=SECRET_KEY,
        same_site="none",
        https_only=True,
        max_age=7200
    )

    # CORS configuration
    app.add_middleware(
        CORSMiddleware,
        allow_origins=[
            "http://localhost:3000",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app",
            "https://*.vercel.app",
        ],
        allow_credentials=True,
        allow_methods=["*"],
        allow_headers=["*"],
    )

    # ========================================================================
    # UPDATED ROUTE DEFINITIONS
    # ========================================================================

    @app.get("/")
    async def root():
        return {"message": "NTU Add-Drop Automator Backend API", "status": "running"}

    @app.post("/api/login")
    async def api_login(request: Request, login_data: LoginRequest, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        if not validate_login(login_data.username, login_data.password):
            raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid credentials")

        # Create secure session in Redis (with combined structure)
        session_id = create_secure_session(redis_db, login_data.username, login_data.password)
        
        # Store only session ID in FastAPI session
        request.session["session_id"] = session_id
        request.session["authenticated"] = True

        return {"success": True, "message": "Login successful"}

    @app.post("/api/submit-swap")
    async def api_submit_swap(request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            form_data = await request.json()

            # Get session ID from session
            session_id = request.session.get("session_id")
            if not session_id:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="No active session")
            
            # Get decrypted credentials from Redis
            username, password = get_decrypted_credentials(redis_db, session_id)
            
            # Validate credentials
            if not validate_login(username, password):
                raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail="Invalid credentials")

            # Parse module data
            num_modules = form_data.get("num_modules", 0)
            module_data = form_data.get("modules", [])

            if num_modules <= 0 or len(module_data) != num_modules:
                raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="Invalid module data")

            # Parse modules into swap_items
            swap_items = []
            for i, module in enumerate(module_data):
                old_index = module.get("old_index")
                new_indexes_raw = module.get("new_indexes")

                if not old_index or not new_indexes_raw:
                    raise HTTPException(
                        status_code=400,
                        detail=f"Missing or invalid data for module {i + 1}",
                    )

                new_index_list = [
                    index.strip()
                    for index in new_indexes_raw.split(",")
                    if index.strip()
                ]

                swap_items.append({
                    "old_index": old_index,
                    "new_indexes": new_index_list,
                    "swapped": False,
                })

            # Initialize swap data in the existing session
            initialize_swap_in_session(redis_db, session_id, swap_items)

            # Start background thread (pass session_id instead of separate swap_id)
            thread = threading.Thread(
                target=perform_swaps,
                args=(username, password, swap_items, session_id, redis_db),
                daemon=True,
            )
            thread.start()

            return {
                "success": True,
                "session_id": session_id,  # Return session_id instead of swap_id
                "message": "Swap process started successfully",
            }

        except HTTPException:
            raise
        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(
                status_code=500, detail=f"Swap initiation failed: {str(e)}"
            )

    @app.get("/api/swap-status/{session_id}")
    async def api_get_swap_status(session_id: str, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        """Get swap status from session data"""
        try:
            session_data = get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id)
            
            # Return swap-specific data
            return {
                "status": session_data.get("swap_status", "idle"),
                "message": session_data.get("swap_message"),
                "details": session_data.get("modules", []),
                "started_at": session_data.get("swap_started_at")
            }
        except HTTPException:
            raise
        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail=f"Error retrieving status: {str(e)}")

    @app.post("/api/stop-swap/{session_id}")
    async def api_stop_swap(session_id: str, request: Request, redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        """Stop swap and clean up session"""
        try:
            # Update swap status to stopped
            update_overall_swap_status(redis_db, session_id, "stopped", "Swap stopped by user")
            
            # Clean up session
            redis_db.delete(f"session:{session_id}")
            request.session.clear()

            return {"success": True, "message": "Swap successfully stopped"}
        except Exception as e:
            raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail=f"Error stopping swap: {str(e)}")

    @app.get("/api/health")
    async def health_check(redis_db=Depends(get_redis)):
        try:
            redis_db.ping()
            return {
                "status": "healthy",
                "redis": "connected",
                "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            }
        except Exception as e:
            return {
                "status": "unhealthy",
                "redis": f"disconnected: {str(e)}",
                "timestamp": datetime.now().isoformat(),
            }

    return app

# ============================================================================
# UPDATED SELENIUM FUNCTIONS
# ============================================================================

def perform_swaps(username, password, swap_items, session_id, redis_db):
    """Updated to use session_id instead of swap_id"""
    driver = None

    try:
        driver = get_driver()
        
        # Update status
        update_overall_swap_status(redis_db, session_id, "processing", "Logging into NTU portal...")
        
        login_success = login_to_portal(driver, username, password, session_id, redis_db)
        if not login_success:
            return

        start_time = time.time()
        
        while True:
            # Check if session still exists (user might have stopped)
            try:
                session_data = get_secure_session(redis_db, session_id)
                if session_data.get("swap_status") == "stopped":
                    break
            except HTTPException:
                break  # Session expired or deleted
            
            # Attempt swaps
            for idx, item in enumerate(swap_items):
                if not item["swapped"]:
                    failed_indexes = []
                    for new_index in item["new_indexes"]:
                        try:
                            success, message = attempt_swap(
                                old_index=item["old_index"],
                                new_index=new_index,
                                idx=idx,
                                driver=driver,
                                session_id=session_id,  # Changed from swap_id
                                redis_db=redis_db,
                            )
                            if success:
                                item["swapped"] = True
                                update_module_status(
                                    redis_db,
                                    session_id,
                                    idx,
                                    f"Successfully swapped {item['old_index']} → {new_index}",
                                    success=True,
                                )
                                break
                            else:
                                failed_indexes.append(new_index)
                        except Exception as e:
                            failed_indexes.append(new_index)
                    
                    if not item["swapped"] and failed_indexes:
                        update_module_status(
                            redis_db,
                            session_id,
                            idx,
                            f"Indexes {', '.join(failed_indexes)} have no vacancies.",
                        )

            # Check completion
            all_swapped = all(item["swapped"] for item in swap_items)
            if all_swapped:
                update_overall_swap_status(
                    redis_db,
                    session_id,
                    "completed",
                    "All modules have been successfully swapped!",
                )
                break

            # Check timeout
            if time.time() - start_time >= 2 * 3600:
                update_overall_swap_status(
                    redis_db,
                    session_id,
                    "timed_out",
                    "Time limit reached before completing the swap.",
                )
                break

            time.sleep(5 * 60)  # Wait 5 minutes

    except Exception as e:
        update_overall_swap_status(
            redis_db, 
            session_id, 
            "error", 
            f"An error occurred: {str(e)}"
        )
    finally:
        if driver:
            release_driver(driver)

# Update attempt_swap function signature
async def attempt_swap(old_index, new_index, idx, driver, session_id, redis_db):
    """Updated to use session_id instead of swap_id"""
    try:
        update_module_status(
            redis_db, session_id, idx, f"Attempting to swap {old_index} → {new_index}"
        )
        
        # ... rest of your existing attempt_swap logic ...
        # Just replace all instances of swap_id with session_id
        
        return True, ""  # Success
    except Exception as e:
        error_message = f"Error during swap attempt for {old_index} → {new_index}: {str(e)}"
        update_module_status(redis_db, session_id, idx, error_message)
        return False, error_message

# ... rest of your existing code ...
````

2. **✅ Better security** - Session ID instead of username-based keys  
3. **✅ Automatic cleanup** - When session expires, everything goes
4. **✅ Simpler code** - No need to manage two separate Redis instances
5. **✅ Better performance** - Fewer Redis calls

def set_status_data(redis_db, swap_id, data):
    redis_db.set(swap_id, json.dumps(data))


def get_status_data(redis_db, swap_id):
    data = redis_db.get(swap_id)
    if data:
        try:
            return json.loads(data)
        except json.JSONDecodeError:
            print(f"Error decoding JSON for swap_id: {swap_id}")
            return {
                "status": "error",
                "details": [],
                "message": "Error retrieving status data",
            }
    return {"status": "idle", "details": [], "message": None}


def update_status(redis_db, swap_id, idx, message, success=False):
    """
    Updates the status of a specific module swap in Redis.

    Args:
        redis_db: Redis database connection.
        swap_id (str): Unique swap session ID.
        idx (int): Index of the module in the details list.
        message (str): Message to update in the status.
        success (bool): Whether the swap was successful.
    """
    status_data = get_status_data(redis_db, swap_id)

    if idx < len(status_data["details"]):
        status_data["details"][idx]["message"] = message
        if success:
            status_data["details"][idx]["swapped"] = True
        redis_db.set(swap_id, json.dumps(status_data))


def update_overall_status(redis_db, swap_id, status, message):
    """
    Updates the overall status and message of the swap operation in Redis.

    Args:
        redis_db: Redis connection (injected via FastAPI).
        swap_id (str): Unique swap session ID.
        status (str): The overall status to set (e.g., "Error", "Completed").
        message (str): The overall message to set.
    """
    status_data = get_status_data(redis_db, swap_id)  # Fetch current status
    status_data["status"] = status  # Update overall status
    status_data["message"] = message  # Update overall message
    set_status_data(redis_db, swap_id, status_data)  # Save changes back to Redis

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  const fetchStatus = async () => {
    if (!sessionId) return;

    try {
      const response = await axios.get(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/swap-status/${sessionId}`, {
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      setSwapData(response.data);
      setError('');
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Error fetching status:', error);
      
      // ✅ Handle session expiration
      if (error.response?.status === 401) {
        setError('Session expired. Redirecting to login...');
        setTimeout(() => navigate('/'), 2000);
        return;
      }
      
      setError('Failed to fetch swap status');
    }
  };
// ...existing code...
````

````javascript
    status: 'Loading',  // ✅ Change from 'Processing' to 'Loading'

````

## Why Keep It:

1. **✅ Prevents undefined errors** - Component renders immediately with safe defaults
2. **✅ Better UX** - Shows "Loading..." instead of blank screen  
3. **✅ Type safety** - Ensures swapData always has expected structure
4. **✅ Handles edge cases** - If API call fails, component still works

## Why Change 'Processing' to 'Loading':

- **'Processing'** implies an actual swap is running
- **'Loading'** correctly indicates we're fetching status
- Prevents confusion if API is slow to respond

## Your Current Flow is Correct:

```
1. Component mounts → Shows "Loading swap status..."
2. useEffect runs → Calls fetchStatus()  
3. API response → setSwapData(response.data) replaces initial state
4. Real status displayed → "Processing", "Completed", etc.
```

josshhz11: once i have installed ruff as a linter:
C:\Users\joshua\Downloads\ntu-add-drop-automator-v2\frontend>ruff --version
ruff 0.14.4

how do i create a workflow for always ensuring and checking (with commands in the CLI) to get my code linted?


GitHub Copilot: Great! Here are several ways to set up automated linting workflows with Ruff:

## 1. Basic CLI Commands for Manual Checking

````bash
# Check all Python files for linting issues
ruff check .

# Check specific file
ruff check backend/app.py

# Fix auto-fixable issues
ruff check --fix .

# Format code (similar to black)
ruff format .

# Check and format in one command
ruff check --fix . && ruff format .
````

## 2. Create NPM Scripts for Easy Access

````json
{
  "scripts": {
    "lint:python": "ruff check ../backend",
    "lint:python:fix": "ruff check --fix ../backend",
    "format:python": "ruff format ../backend",
    "lint:all": "ruff check ../backend && npm run lint",
    "fix:all": "ruff check --fix ../backend && ruff format ../backend && npm run lint:fix"
  }
}
````

Then run:
```bash
npm run lint:python
npm run fix:all
```

## 3. Create Ruff Configuration File

````toml
[tool.ruff]
# Same as Black's line length
line-length = 88

# Enable specific rule sets
select = [
    "E",   # pycodestyle errors
    "W",   # pycodestyle warnings  
    "F",   # Pyflakes
    "I",   # isort
    "B",   # flake8-bugbear
    "C4",  # flake8-comprehensions
    "UP",  # pyupgrade
]

# Ignore specific rules
ignore = [
    "E501",  # line too long (handled by formatter)
    "B008",  # do not perform function calls in argument defaults
]

# Exclude directories
exclude = [
    ".git",
    "__pycache__",
    ".venv",
    "venv",
    "node_modules",
]

# Target Python 3.9+
target-version = "py39"

[tool.ruff.format]
# Use double quotes for strings
quote-style = "double"

# Indent with spaces
indent-style = "space"
````

## 4. Pre-commit Hook (Recommended)

Install pre-commit:
```bash
pip install pre-commit
```

Create pre-commit config:
````yaml
repos:
  - repo: https://github.com/astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit
    rev: v0.1.6
    hooks:
      - id: ruff
        args: [--fix]
      - id: ruff-format
  
  # Optional: Add frontend linting too
  - repo: local
    hooks:
      - id: eslint
        name: ESLint
        entry: bash -c 'cd frontend && npm run lint'
        language: system
        files: \.(js|jsx|ts|tsx)$
        pass_filenames: false
````

Install the hooks:
```bash
pre-commit install
```

Now ruff runs automatically on every commit!

## 5. GitHub Actions Workflow

````yaml
name: Lint and Format

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  lint-python:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Python
      uses: actions/setup-python@v4
      with:
        python-version: '3.9'
    
    - name: Install Ruff
      run: pip install ruff

    - name: Run Ruff Linter
      run: ruff check backend/

    - name: Run Ruff Formatter Check
      run: ruff format --check backend/

  lint-frontend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Node.js
      uses: actions/setup-node@v4
      with:
        node-version: '18'
        
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: cd frontend && npm install
      
    - name: Run ESLint
      run: cd frontend && npm run lint
````

## 6. VS Code Integration

````json
{
  "python.linting.enabled": true,
  "python.linting.ruffEnabled": true,
  "python.formatting.provider": "none",
  "python.defaultInterpreterPath": "./backend/venv/bin/python",
  
  "[python]": {
    "editor.defaultFormatter": "charliermarsh.ruff",
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.ruff": true,
      "source.organizeImports.ruff": true
    }
  }
}
````

## 7. Makefile for Easy Commands

````makefile
# Python linting and formatting
lint-python:
	ruff check backend/

fix-python:
	ruff check --fix backend/
	ruff format backend/

# Frontend linting
lint-frontend:
	cd frontend && npm run lint

fix-frontend:
	cd frontend && npm run lint:fix

# Combined commands
lint-all: lint-python lint-frontend

fix-all: fix-python fix-frontend

# Check before commit
pre-commit: fix-all
	@echo "Code formatted and linted!"

.PHONY: lint-python fix-python lint-frontend fix-frontend lint-all fix-all pre-commit
````

Then run:
```bash
make lint-all
make fix-all
make pre-commit
```

## 8. Daily Workflow Commands

Here's your daily routine:

```bash
# Before starting work
make lint-all

# While coding (frequent checks)
ruff check backend/app.py

# Before committing
make fix-all
git add .
git commit -m "Your commit message"
# (pre-commit hook runs automatically)

# Quick fix during development
ruff check --fix backend/ && ruff format backend/
```

## Recommended Setup:

1. **Create the `pyproject.toml`** configuration
2. **Install pre-commit hooks** for automatic checking
3. **Add GitHub Actions** for CI/CD
4. **Use VS Code extension** for real-time feedback
5. **Create Makefile** for easy CLI commands

This gives you both automated checking and manual control when needed!

josshhz11: can you explain the above steps, what each step does, and in detail how each step contributes to the overall workflow of automated linting

GitHub Copilot: I'll explain each linting step and how they work together to create a comprehensive automated workflow:

## 1. Basic CLI Commands (Manual Foundation)

### What it does:
```bash
# Check for issues without fixing
ruff check .

# Auto-fix simple issues (imports, formatting)
ruff check --fix .

# Format code (like black formatter)
ruff format .
```

### How it contributes:
- **Foundation**: Provides immediate feedback during development
- **Quick fixes**: Automatically resolves common issues (unused imports, spacing)
- **Manual control**: Developers can run when needed
- **Learning**: Shows what issues exist before automation kicks in

## 2. NPM Scripts Integration

### What it does:
```json
{
  "scripts": {
    "lint:python": "ruff check ../backend",
    "fix:all": "ruff check --fix ../backend && ruff format ../backend"
  }
}
```

### How it contributes:
- **Unified interface**: Frontend developers can lint Python code using familiar `npm run` commands
- **Cross-project consistency**: Same commands work across different parts of your stack
- **Convenience**: Shorter commands (`npm run fix:all` vs long ruff commands)
- **Team standardization**: Everyone uses same commands regardless of their background

## 3. Ruff Configuration File (`pyproject.toml`)

### What it does:
```toml
[tool.ruff]
line-length = 88
select = ["E", "W", "F", "I", "B", "C4", "UP"]  # Enable rule categories
ignore = ["E501", "B008"]                        # Ignore specific rules
exclude = [".git", "__pycache__", ".venv"]      # Skip directories
target-version = "py39"                         # Python version target
```

### How it contributes:
- **Consistency**: Same rules applied everywhere (local, CI/CD, team members)
- **Customization**: Tailored to your project's needs and coding standards
- **Performance**: Excludes irrelevant directories, speeds up linting
- **Documentation**: Configuration is version-controlled and shared with team

### Rule Categories Explained:
- **E, W**: pycodestyle errors/warnings (PEP 8 compliance)
- **F**: Pyflakes (unused imports, undefined variables)
- **I**: isort (import sorting and grouping)
- **B**: flake8-bugbear (common bugs and design problems)
- **C4**: flake8-comprehensions (better list/dict comprehensions)
- **UP**: pyupgrade (modernize Python syntax)

## 4. Pre-commit Hooks (Git Integration)

### What it does:
```yaml
repos:
  - repo: https://github.com/astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit
    rev: v0.1.6
    hooks:
      - id: ruff
        args: [--fix]      # Auto-fix on commit
      - id: ruff-format    # Format on commit
```

### How it contributes:
- **Prevention**: Stops bad code from entering the repository
- **Automatic**: Runs without developer remembering
- **Fast feedback**: Catches issues at commit time (earliest possible)
- **Consistency**: Ensures all commits meet standards
- **Time-saving**: Auto-fixes simple issues before commit completes

### Workflow:
```bash
git add .
git commit -m "Add feature"
# → Pre-commit runs automatically
# → Ruff checks and fixes code
# → If fixes made, commit is stopped
# → Developer reviews changes and re-commits
```

## 5. GitHub Actions (CI/CD Integration)

### What it does:
```yaml
jobs:
  lint-python:
    steps:
    - name: Run Ruff Linter
      run: ruff check backend/
    - name: Run Ruff Formatter Check  
      run: ruff format --check backend/
```

### How it contributes:
- **Quality gate**: Prevents merging of non-compliant code
- **Team enforcement**: Applies to all contributors, including external ones
- **Visibility**: Shows lint status in pull requests
- **Automation**: Runs on every push/PR without manual intervention
- **Documentation**: Lint results are recorded and visible in PR history

### Protection levels:
1. **Local pre-commit**: Catches 90% of issues
2. **CI/CD**: Catches remaining 10% (bypass protection, different environments)
3. **PR reviews**: Final human check for logic/design

## 6. VS Code Integration (Real-time Feedback)

### What it does:
```json
{
  "[python]": {
    "editor.defaultFormatter": "charliermarsh.ruff",
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.ruff": true
    }
  }
}
```

### How it contributes:
- **Real-time**: Shows issues as you type (red squiggles)
- **Proactive**: Fixes issues before you even save the file
- **Learning**: Developers see and learn from corrections immediately
- **Productivity**: No context switching to terminal for linting
- **Prevention**: Issues caught before they reach git

### Developer experience:
```
Type code → See issue underlined → Auto-fix on save → Clean code committed
```

## 7. Makefile (Simplified Commands)

### What it does:
```makefile
lint-all: lint-python lint-frontend
fix-all: fix-python fix-frontend
pre-commit: fix-all
```

### How it contributes:
- **Simplicity**: One command does everything (`make fix-all`)
- **Consistency**: Same commands across different projects
- **Coordination**: Combines frontend and backend linting
- **Documentation**: Self-documenting workflow (`make` shows available commands)

## 8. Complete Automated Workflow

Here's how all pieces work together in practice:

### During Development:
```
1. Developer types code
   ↓
2. VS Code shows real-time issues
   ↓
3. Auto-fix on save
   ↓
4. Clean code ready for commit
```

### During Commit:
```
1. Developer runs: git commit
   ↓
2. Pre-commit hook runs automatically
   ↓
3. Ruff checks and fixes code
   ↓
4. If changes made: commit stopped, developer reviews
   ↓
5. Developer re-commits clean code
```

### During Push/PR:
```
1. Code pushed to GitHub
   ↓
2. GitHub Actions triggered
   ↓
3. CI runs full lint check
   ↓
4. Pass: PR can be merged
   Fail: PR blocked until fixed
```

## Benefits of This Layered Approach:

### 🛡️ **Defense in Depth**
- **Layer 1**: VS Code (real-time)
- **Layer 2**: Pre-commit (git-level)  
- **Layer 3**: CI/CD (repository-level)

### ⚡ **Performance Optimization**
- Most issues caught early (fast feedback)
- Fewer issues reach expensive CI/CD stage
- Developers learn and make fewer mistakes over time

### 👥 **Team Consistency**
- Same standards enforced for everyone
- Configuration shared via git
- No "it works on my machine" problems

### 🔄 **Continuous Improvement**
- Linting rules evolve with your codebase
- Team learns best practices through automated suggestions
- Code quality improves over time without manual effort

## For Your NTU Project Specifically:

Given your backend code, here's what each layer would catch:

```python
# VS Code would show real-time:
- Unused imports
- Long lines
- Missing docstrings

# Pre-commit would fix:
- Import sorting
- Trailing whitespace  
- Basic formatting

# CI would catch:
- Complex style issues
- Cross-file inconsistencies
- Issues that bypass local tools
```

This creates a robust, automated system that maintains code quality without slowing down development.

GitHub Copilot: I'll walk you through setting up a complete automated linting workflow for your NTU project. Here's everything step by step:

## Step 1: Install Ruff Locally

````bash
# In your project root directory
cd C:\Users\joshua\Downloads\ntu-add-drop-automator-v2

# Install ruff globally (you already have this)
pip install ruff

# Verify installation
ruff --version
````

## Step 2: Create Ruff Configuration

Create a configuration file for consistent linting rules:

````toml
[tool.ruff]
# Same as Black's line length
line-length = 88

# Enable specific rule sets
select = [
    "E",   # pycodestyle errors
    "W",   # pycodestyle warnings  
    "F",   # Pyflakes
    "I",   # isort (import sorting)
    "B",   # flake8-bugbear
    "C4",  # flake8-comprehensions
    "UP",  # pyupgrade (modernize syntax)
    "N",   # pep8-naming
]

# Ignore specific rules that might be too strict
ignore = [
    "E501",  # line too long (handled by formatter)
    "B008",  # do not perform function calls in argument defaults
    "E203",  # whitespace before ':'
    "W503",  # line break before binary operator
]

# Exclude directories from linting
exclude = [
    ".git",
    "__pycache__",
    ".venv",
    "venv",
    "node_modules",
    "build",
    "dist",
    ".pytest_cache",
]

# Target Python 3.9+ (match your project)
target-version = "py39"

[tool.ruff.format]
# Use double quotes for strings
quote-style = "double"

# Indent with 4 spaces
indent-style = "space"

# Skip string normalization to avoid conflicts
skip-string-normalization = false

[tool.ruff.isort]
# Group imports
known-first-party = ["app"]
force-single-line = false
````

## Step 3: Install Pre-commit

````bash
# Install pre-commit
pip install pre-commit

# Add to your requirements.txt for team consistency
echo pre-commit >> backend/requirements-dev.txt
````

## Step 4: Create Pre-commit Configuration

````yaml
repos:
  # Ruff linting and formatting
  - repo: https://github.com/astral-sh/ruff-pre-commit
    rev: v0.1.6
    hooks:
      - id: ruff
        name: Ruff Linter
        args: [--fix, --exit-non-zero-on-fix]
        files: ^backend/.*\.py$
      - id: ruff-format
        name: Ruff Formatter
        files: ^backend/.*\.py$

  # Frontend linting (optional)
  - repo: local
    hooks:
      - id: eslint
        name: ESLint
        entry: bash -c 'cd frontend && npm run lint --fix'
        language: system
        files: \.(js|jsx|ts|tsx)$
        pass_filenames: false

  # General file checks
  - repo: https://github.com/pre-commit/pre-commit-hooks
    rev: v4.5.0
    hooks:
      - id: trailing-whitespace
      - id: end-of-file-fixer
      - id: check-yaml
      - id: check-json
      - id: check-merge-conflict
````

## Step 5: Install Pre-commit Hooks

````bash
# Install the git hooks
pre-commit install

# Test the hooks on all files (optional)
pre-commit run --all-files
````

## Step 6: Create Makefile for Easy Commands

````makefile
.PHONY: lint-python fix-python lint-frontend fix-frontend lint-all fix-all install-deps test pre-commit

# Python linting
lint-python:
	@echo "Linting Python code..."
	ruff check backend/

fix-python:
	@echo "Fixing Python code..."
	ruff check --fix backend/
	ruff format backend/

# Frontend linting
lint-frontend:
	@echo "Linting frontend code..."
	cd frontend && npm run lint

fix-frontend:
	@echo "Fixing frontend code..."
	cd frontend && npm run lint:fix

# Combined commands
lint-all: lint-python lint-frontend
	@echo "All linting complete!"

fix-all: fix-python fix-frontend
	@echo "All fixes applied!"

# Development setup
install-deps:
	@echo "Installing Python dependencies..."
	pip install -r backend/requirements.txt
	pip install -r backend/requirements-dev.txt
	@echo "Installing frontend dependencies..."
	cd frontend && npm install

# Testing
test:
	@echo "Running Python tests..."
	cd backend && python -m pytest
	@echo "Running frontend tests..."
	cd frontend && npm test

# Pre-commit checks
pre-commit: fix-all
	@echo "Running pre-commit checks..."
	pre-commit run --all-files
	@echo "Ready to commit!"

# Help
help:
	@echo "Available commands:"
	@echo "  lint-python    - Check Python code for issues"
	@echo "  fix-python     - Fix Python code issues automatically"
	@echo "  lint-frontend  - Check frontend code for issues"
	@echo "  fix-frontend   - Fix frontend code issues automatically"
	@echo "  lint-all       - Check all code"
	@echo "  fix-all        - Fix all code issues"
	@echo "  install-deps   - Install all dependencies"
	@echo "  test           - Run all tests"
	@echo "  pre-commit     - Run all checks before committing"
````

## Step 7: Update NPM Scripts

````json
{
  "scripts": {
    "start": "react-scripts start",
    "build": "react-scripts build",
    "test": "react-scripts test",
    "eject": "react-scripts eject",
    "lint": "eslint src --ext .js,.jsx,.ts,.tsx",
    "lint:fix": "eslint src --ext .js,.jsx,.ts,.tsx --fix",
    "lint:python": "ruff check ../backend",
    "lint:python:fix": "ruff check --fix ../backend && ruff format ../backend"
  }
}
````

## Step 8: Create GitHub Actions Workflow

````yaml
name: Lint and Test

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  lint-python:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Python 3.9
      uses: actions/setup-python@v4
      with:
        python-version: '3.9'
    
    - name: Cache pip dependencies
      uses: actions/cache@v3
      with:
        path: ~/.cache/pip
        key: ${{ runner.os }}-pip-${{ hashFiles('**/requirements*.txt') }}
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: |
        python -m pip install --upgrade pip
        pip install ruff
    
    - name: Run Ruff Linter
      run: ruff check backend/ --output-format=github
    
    - name: Run Ruff Formatter Check
      run: ruff format --check backend/

  lint-frontend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Node.js
      uses: actions/setup-node@v4
      with:
        node-version: '18'
        cache: 'npm'
        cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: cd frontend && npm ci
    
    - name: Run ESLint
      run: cd frontend && npm run lint

  test-backend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: lint-python
    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Python 3.9
      uses: actions/setup-python@v4
      with:
        python-version: '3.9'
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: |
        python -m pip install --upgrade pip
        pip install -r backend/requirements.txt
        pip install pytest pytest-asyncio httpx
    
    - name: Run tests
      run: cd backend && python -m pytest

  test-frontend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: lint-frontend
    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Node.js
      uses: actions/setup-node@v4
      with:
        node-version: '18'
        cache: 'npm'
        cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: cd frontend && npm ci
    
    - name: Run tests
      run: cd frontend && npm test -- --coverage --watchAll=false
````

````
# Development dependencies
ruff==0.1.6
pre-commit==3.5.0
pytest==7.4.3
pytest-asyncio==0.21.1

httpx==0.25.2
fakeredis==2.20.0
````

````json
  "python.linting.ruffEnabled": true,
  "python.formatting.provider": "none",
  
  "[python]": {
    "editor.defaultFormatter": "charliermarsh.ruff",
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.ruff": true,
      "source.organizeImports.ruff": true
    }
  },
  
  "files.associations": {
    "*.toml": "toml"
  },
  
  "eslint.workingDirectories": ["frontend"],
  
  "[javascript]": {
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.eslint": true
    }
  },
  
  "[javascriptreact]": {
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.eslint": true
    }
  }
}
````

````json
  "python.linting.ruffEnabled": true,
  "python.formatting.provider": "none",
  
  "[python]": {
    "editor.defaultFormatter": "charliermarsh.ruff",
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.ruff": true,
      "source.organizeImports.ruff": true
    }
  },
  
  "files.associations": {
    "*.toml": "toml"
  },
  
  "eslint.workingDirectories": ["frontend"],
  
  "[javascript]": {
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.eslint": true
    }
  },
  
  "[javascriptreact]": {
    "editor.formatOnSave": true,
    "editor.codeActionsOnSave": {
      "source.fixAll.eslint": true
    }
  }
}
````

josshhz11: then what is the difference between .pre-commit-config.yaml and lint-and-test.yml.

lint-and-test.yml is a github actions workflow right? and what about Makefile? are these overkill? i want it to be minimal

GitHub Copilot: Great question! Let me clarify the differences and help you decide what's minimal:

## Key Differences:

### 1. .pre-commit-config.yaml (Local - Your Machine)
```
Runs on: Your computer
When: When you try to commit code (git commit)
Purpose: Prevents bad code from entering git history
Action: Blocks commit if issues found, auto-fixes them
```

### 2. lint-and-test.yml (Remote - GitHub)
```
Runs on: GitHub's servers
When: When you push code or create a pull request
Purpose: Final quality gate before merging
Action: Blocks PR merge if issues found
```

### 3. Makefile (Helper - Your Machine)
```
Runs on: Your computer
When: You manually run commands
Purpose: Convenient shortcuts for common tasks
Action: Just runs commands you tell it to
```

## Visual Workflow:

```
You write code
    ↓
You commit (git commit)
    ↓ [Pre-commit runs] ← .pre-commit-config.yaml
    ├─ Issues? → Auto-fix → You review → Re-commit
    └─ No issues? → Commit succeeds
    ↓
You push (git push)
    ↓ [GitHub Actions runs] ← lint-and-test.yml
    ├─ Issues? → PR blocked, can't merge
    └─ No issues? → PR can merge
```

## Are These Overkill? (Minimal Setup)

**No, not overkill.** Here's what you actually need:

### Minimal Option 1: Just Pre-commit (70% Protection)
```
Keep:
  ✅ .pre-commit-config.yaml
  
Remove:
  ❌ lint-and-test.yml
  ❌ Makefile
```

**Pros:**
- Catches issues locally before pushing
- Prevents mistakes from reaching GitHub
- Minimal setup

**Cons:**
- No safety net if someone bypasses pre-commit
- Developers could force-push bad code

### Minimal Option 2: Pre-commit + GitHub Actions (100% Protection) ⭐ Recommended
```
Keep:
  ✅ .pre-commit-config.yaml
  ✅ lint-and-test.yml
  
Remove:
  ❌ Makefile
```

**Pros:**
- Local protection (pre-commit)
- Repository protection (GitHub Actions)
- No way for bad code to merge
- Team-wide enforcement

**Cons:**
- Slightly more setup

### Minimal Option 3: Just Manual Commands (No Automation)
```
Keep:
  ✅ Makefile (or just use ruff commands directly)
  
Remove:
  ❌ .pre-commit-config.yaml
  ❌ lint-and-test.yml
```

**Pros:**
- Simplest setup
- No hooks to manage

**Cons:**
- Relies on developers remembering to lint
- Bad code can accidentally get committed
- No team enforcement

## My Recommendation for Your Project:

**Use Option 2: Pre-commit + GitHub Actions**

Why?
- You're **open-sourcing** this project
- You want **consistency** for external contributors
- GitHub Actions **blocks bad PRs** automatically
- Pre-commit is **lightweight** (minimal overhead)

josshhz11: then for lint-and-test.yml, this is a github actions workflow that will automatically be set up once it is pushed to the github repo? i don't have to configure anything under "Actions" right? in the repo?

also what is the difference between .yaml and .yml files? especially for the lint-and-test.yml and the .pre-commit-config.yaml. when is each used and for what purpose?

GitHub Copilot: Great questions! Let me clarify both:

## GitHub Actions - Automatic Setup

**YES, it's completely automatic!** ✅

Once you push lint-and-test.yml to your GitHub repo:

1. GitHub automatically detects it
2. Workflow activates immediately
3. Runs on every push and PR
4. **No configuration needed** in the repo settings

You don't need to do anything in the "Actions" tab—it just works!

### How to verify it's working:

1. Push your code to GitHub
2. Go to your repo → **Actions** tab
3. You'll see your workflow running
4. If it fails, GitHub shows you why in the PR

## `.yaml` vs `.yml` - They're The Same! 

**`.yaml` and `.yml` are identical.** Both are valid file extensions for YAML files.

- **`.yaml`** = Long form (more explicit)
- **`.yml`** = Short form (more compact)

### When Each Is Used:

For your project specifically:

```
.pre-commit-config.yaml    ← Uses .yaml (convention for pre-commit)
lint-and-test.yml          ← Uses .yml (convention for GitHub Actions)
```

**But honestly, either would work for both files.** The difference is just convention:

- **Pre-commit tools** traditionally use `.yaml`
- **GitHub Actions tools** traditionally use `.yml`

You could rename them:
- `.pre-commit-config.yml` ✅ (still works)
- `lint-and-test.yaml` ✅ (still works)

### Why the Different Conventions?

It's just **community standards** that evolved over time:
- Pre-commit community preferred `.yaml`
- GitHub Actions community preferred `.yml`

Both are YAML format, same syntax, same functionality.

## Your Setup Breakdown:

```
.pre-commit-config.yaml
├─ Format: YAML
├─ Location: Project root
├─ Runs on: Your local machine
├─ When: When you commit (git commit)
└─ Purpose: Prevent bad code from entering git

.github/workflows/lint-and-test.yml
├─ Format: YAML (same as .yaml)
├─ Location: .github/workflows/
├─ Runs on: GitHub servers
├─ When: When you push or create PR
└─ Purpose: Final check before merging
```

## Complete Workflow (Both Files Working Together):

```
1. You write code on your machine
   ↓
2. You run: git commit -m "message"
   ↓
3. .pre-commit-config.yaml runs (Local)
   ├─ Lints Python code
   ├─ Auto-fixes issues
   ├─ If issues remain → Commit blocked
   └─ If no issues → Commit succeeds
   ↓
4. You run: git push
   ↓
5. Code goes to GitHub
   ↓
6. lint-and-test.yml runs (Remote on GitHub servers)
   ├─ Lints Python code again
   ├─ Lints frontend code
   ├─ If fails → PR shows as failed
   └─ If passes → PR shows as passed
   ↓
7. PR can be merged (if it passes)
```

## What You Need to Do:

### Nothing! Just push to GitHub:


josshhz11: then do i still need pyproject.toml?

GitHub Copilot: **YES, you still need pyproject.toml!** ✅

Here's why:

## What pyproject.toml Does:

It tells Ruff **how to lint your code** (the rules and settings).

Without it, Ruff uses default settings, which might not match your project's style.

## Comparison:

### Without pyproject.toml:
```bash
ruff check backend/
# Uses Ruff's default settings
# Might be too strict or too lenient for your project
```

### With pyproject.toml:
```bash
ruff check backend/
# Uses YOUR custom settings from pyproject.toml
# Line length 88, ignore E501, etc.
# Consistent across your team
```

## Where They Work Together:

```
Your files:
├── backend/
│   ├── app.py
│   ├── pyproject.toml          ← Ruff reads THIS for rules
│   └── requirements.txt
├── .pre-commit-config.yaml     ← Runs ruff (uses pyproject.toml)
└── .github/workflows/
    └── lint-and-test.yml       ← Runs ruff (uses pyproject.toml)

Both pre-commit and GitHub Actions will read your pyproject.toml!
```

## Example of What pyproject.toml Controls:

Without it, these would be different:
```python
# With pyproject.toml (line-length: 88)
x = "this is a very long line that exceeds 88 characters but is under default Ruff limit"
# ✅ Passes

# Without pyproject.toml (default line-length: 88)
x = "this is a very long line that exceeds 88 characters but is under default Ruff limit"
# ❌ Might fail with different defaults
```

## Do You NEED It?

**Technically no**, but **practically yes**:

- ❌ Without it: Works but inconsistent
- ✅ With it: Works and consistent

## Keep It Simple:

Your pyproject.toml is already good! Just keep it as-is in backend folder:

```
backend/
├── app.py
├── requirements.txt
└── pyproject.toml     ← Keep this!
```

## File Checklist:

```
Keep and push to GitHub:
✅ backend/pyproject.toml
✅ .pre-commit-config.yaml
✅ .github/workflows/lint-and-test.yml
✅ backend/requirements.txt (with ruff added)

Remove (optional):
❌ Makefile
❌ .vscode/settings.json
```

**Bottom line:** pyproject.toml is the "rulebook" for Ruff. Both pre-commit and GitHub Actions use it. Definitely keep it! 📋

josshhz11: im getting this error caused by pyproject.toml and the syntax. how do i fix this?

GitHub Copilot: Looking at the error, there's a syntax issue in your pyproject.toml file. The error shows:

```
unknown field 'skip-string-normalization', expected one of 'exclude', 'preview', 'indent-style', 'quote-style', 'skip-magic-trailing-comma', 'line-ending', 'docstring-code-format', 'docstring-code-line-length'
```

## Fix: Update your pyproject.toml

The field `skip-string-normalization` doesn't exist in Ruff. Here's the corrected version:

````toml
[tool.ruff]
# Same as Black's line length
line-length = 88

# Enable specific rule sets
select = [
    "E",   # pycodestyle errors
    "W",   # pycodestyle warnings  
    "F",   # Pyflakes
    "I",   # isort (import sorting)
    "B",   # flake8-bugbear
    "C4",  # flake8-comprehensions
    "UP",  # pyupgrade (modernize syntax)
    "N",   # pep8-naming
]

# Ignore specific rules that might be too strict
ignore = [
    "E501",  # line too long (handled by formatter)
    "B008",  # do not perform function calls in argument defaults
    "E203",  # whitespace before ':'
    "W503",  # line break before binary operator
]

# Exclude directories from linting
exclude = [
    ".git",
    "__pycache__",
    ".venv",
    "venv",
    "node_modules",
    "build",
    "dist",
    ".pytest_cache",
]

# Target Python 3.9+ (match your project)
target-version = "py39"

[tool.ruff.format]
# Use double quotes for strings
quote-style = "double"

# Indent with 4 spaces
indent-style = "space"

[tool.ruff.isort]
# Group imports
known-first-party = ["app"]
````

## What I Changed:

1. **Removed** `skip-string-normalization = false` (this field doesn't exist in Ruff)
2. **Removed** `force-single-line = false` (moved from `[tool.ruff.isort]` section as it's not needed)

## Test the Fix:

````bash

# If no errors, commit and push
git add backend/pyproject.toml
git commit -m "Fix pyproject.toml syntax error"
git push
````

josshhz11: how do i fix these errors?

GitHub Copilot: Looking at your errors, I can see two ESLint issues in your SwapStatus.js file:

## Fix 1: Remove Unused Variable

**Error:** `'numModules' is assigned a value but never used`

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  // Get data passed from InputIndex or use placeholder data
  const passedData = location.state || {};
  const sessionId = passedData.session_id;
  // Remove this line - it's not being used anywhere
  // const numModules = passedData.numModules || 1; // Default to 1

````

## Fix 2: Add Missing Dependency to useEffect

**Error:** React Hook useEffect has a missing dependency: 'fetchStatus'

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  // Set up polling for status updates
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!sessionId) {
      setError('No session ID provided');
      return;
    }

    // Fetch swap_status immediately
    fetchStatus();

    // Set up polling every 5 seconds
    const interval = setInterval(fetchStatus, 5000);
    
    // Cleanup interval on component unmount
    return () => clearInterval(interval);
  }, [sessionId, fetchStatus]); // ✅ Add fetchStatus to dependencies
// ...existing code...
````

## Fix 3: Also Fix the Backend Route Issue

Your frontend is calling `/api/logout/${sessionId}` but your backend doesn't have this route. Change it to use the existing stop-swap route:

````javascript
// ...existing code...
  // Log out is for after successful swap
  const handleLogout = async () => {
    if (!sessionId) {
      navigate('/');
      return;
    }
    
    setLoading(true);
    try {
      // ✅ Use stop-swap instead of logout (which doesn't exist in backend)
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/stop-swap/${sessionId}`, {}, {
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      if (response.data.success) {
        navigate('/');
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Error during logout:', error);
      navigate('/') // Navigate back to home anyway
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };
// ...existing code...
````

## Complete Fixed Code:

Here's the complete fixed section:

````javascript
const SwapStatus = () => {
  const location = useLocation();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  
  // Get data passed from InputIndex or use placeholder data
  const passedData = location.state || {};
  const sessionId = passedData.session_id;
  // ✅ Removed unused numModules variable
  
  // Initial state - will be replaced by real data from the backend
  const [swapData, setSwapData] = useState({
    status: 'Loading', // Processing, Completed, Error, Timed Out, Stopped
    message: 'Loading swap status...',
    details: []
  });
  const [error, setError] = useState('');
  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(false);

  // Fetch status from backend API call
  const fetchStatus = async () => {
    if (!sessionId) return;

    try {
      const response = await axios.get(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/swap-status/${sessionId}`, {
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      setSwapData(response.data);
      setError('');
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Error fetching status:', error);

      if (error.response?.status === 401) {
        setError('Session expired. Redirecting to login...');
        setTimeout(() => navigate('/'), 2000);
        return;
      }

      setError('Failed to fetch swap status');
    }
  };

  // Set up polling for status updates
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!sessionId) {
      setError('No session ID provided');
      return;
    }

    // Fetch swap_status immediately
    fetchStatus();

    // Set up polling every 5 seconds
    const interval = setInterval(fetchStatus, 5000);
    
    // Cleanup interval on component unmount
    return () => clearInterval(interval);
  }, [sessionId, fetchStatus]); // ✅ Added fetchStatus dependency

  // ... rest of your existing code ...

  // Log out is for after successful swap
  const handleLogout = async () => {
    if (!sessionId) {
      navigate('/');
      return;
    }
    
    setLoading(true);
    try {
      // ✅ Changed from /api/logout to /api/stop-swap
      const response = await axios.post(`${API_BASE_URL}/api/stop-swap/${sessionId}`, {}, {
        withCredentials: true,
      });

      if (response.data.success) {
        navigate('/');
      }
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('Error during logout:', error);
      navigate('/'); // Navigate back to home anyway
    } finally {
      setLoading(false);
    }
  };

  // ... rest of your existing code remains the same ...
````

````bash
# Should show no errors now
````

josshhz11: do you know what is causing this error for the lint-frontend to keep failing?

GitHub Copilot: Looking at the error logs, the issue is that **npm can't install packages because it requires Node.js version >=20.0.0, but your GitHub Actions is using Node.js 18**.

The error shows:
```
npm warn EBADENGINE Unsupported engine {
npm warn EBADENGINE   package: 'react@19.2.0',
npm warn EBADENGINE   required: { node: '>=20.0.0' },
npm warn EBADENGINE   current: { node: 'v18.20.5', npm: '10.8.2' }
}
```

## Fix: Update Node.js Version in GitHub Actions

````yaml
name: Lint and Test

on:
  push:
    branches: [ main, develop ]
  pull_request:
    branches: [ main ]

jobs:
  lint-python:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Python 3.9
      uses: actions/setup-python@v4
      with:
        python-version: '3.9'
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: |
        python -m pip install --upgrade pip
        pip install ruff
    
    - name: Run Ruff Linter
      run: ruff check backend/ --output-format=github
    
    - name: Run Ruff Formatter Check
      run: ruff format --check backend/

  lint-frontend:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
    - name: Checkout code
      uses: actions/checkout@v4
    
    - name: Set up Node.js
      uses: actions/setup-node@v4
      with:
        node-version: '20'  # ✅ Changed from '18' to '20'
        cache: 'npm'
        cache-dependency-path: frontend/package-lock.json
    
    - name: Install dependencies
      run: cd frontend && npm ci
    
    - name: Run ESLint
      run: cd frontend && npm run lint
````

## Why This Happened:

Your package.json uses **React 19.2.0**, which requires Node.js >=20.0.0, but your GitHub Actions was configured to use Node.js 18.

## Alternative Solutions:

### Option 1: Downgrade React (Not Recommended)
You could downgrade to React 18, but you'd lose React 19 features.

### Option 2: Use Latest Node.js (Recommended)
````yaml
node-version: 'latest'  # Uses the most recent stable version
````

### Option 3: Use Specific Node.js 20+ Version
````yaml
node-version: '20.x'    # Uses latest Node.js 20.x version
````

After making this change, commit and push:

````bash
git add .github/workflows/lint-and-test.yml
git commit -m "Update Node.js version to 20 for React 19 compatibility"
git push
````

The frontend linting should now pass! The issue was a **version compatibility mismatch**, not a problem with your code or linting setup.

GitHub Copilot: Excellent question! Let me break down the benefits of migrating to Spring Boot and outline the migration process.

## Benefits of Java Spring Boot over Python FastAPI

### Performance & Scalability
- **🚀 Better concurrency**: Java's JVM threading vs Python's GIL limitations
- **💾 Memory efficiency**: JVM's optimized garbage collection
- **⚡ Faster execution**: Compiled bytecode vs interpreted Python
- **📈 Better horizontal scaling**: JVM's mature ecosystem for clustering

### Enterprise & Stability
- **🏢 Enterprise adoption**: Spring Boot is industry standard for large applications
- **🔒 Mature security**: Spring Security is battle-tested
- **📊 Better monitoring**: Rich ecosystem (Micrometer, Actuator, APM tools)
- **🛡️ Type safety**: Compile-time error detection vs runtime errors in Python

### NTU Alignment
- **👨‍💻 Team consistency**: Matches NTU's existing Java codebase
- **📚 Knowledge sharing**: Easier for Java developers to maintain
- **🔧 Tooling**: Better IDE support, debugging, profiling tools
- **📖 Documentation**: Extensive Spring ecosystem documentation

### Long-term Maintenance
- **🔄 Refactoring**: Better IDE support for large-scale refactoring
- **🧪 Testing**: Mature testing frameworks (JUnit, Mockito, TestContainers)
- **📦 Dependency management**: Maven/Gradle vs pip dependency hell
- **🏗️ Architecture**: Better support for microservices patterns
