### 2. Auth Controller (Equivalent to your FastAPI routes)
````java
package com.ntu.addrop.controller;

import com.ntu.addrop.model.dto.LoginRequest;
import com.ntu.addrop.service.AuthService;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import javax.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;
import javax.validation.Valid;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
@CrossOrigin(origins = {"http://localhost:3000", "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app"})
public class AuthController {

    @Autowired
    private AuthService authService;

    @PostMapping("/login")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> login(
            @Valid @RequestBody LoginRequest loginRequest,
            HttpServletRequest request) {
        
        String sessionId = authService.createSecureSession(
            loginRequest.getUsername(), 
            loginRequest.getPassword()
        );
        
        // Store in HTTP session
        request.getSession().setAttribute("sessionId", sessionId);
        request.getSession().setAttribute("authenticated", true);
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
            "success", true,
            "message", "Login successful"
        ));
    }

    @PostMapping("/logout/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> logout(
            @PathVariable String sessionId,
            HttpServletRequest request) {
        
        authService.cleanupSession(sessionId);
        request.getSession().invalidate();
        
        return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
            "success", true,
            "message", "Logged out successfully"
        ));
    }
}
````

### 3. Session Service (Redis operations)
````java
package com.ntu.addrop.service;

import com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper;
import com.ntu.addrop.model.entity.SessionData;
import com.ntu.addrop.security.EncryptionService;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.concurrent.ThreadLocalRandom;

@Service
public class SessionService {

    @Autowired
    private RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private EncryptionService encryptionService;
    
    @Autowired
    private ObjectMapper objectMapper;

    private static final Duration SESSION_TTL = Duration.ofHours(2);

    public String createSecureSession(String username, String password) {
        String sessionId = generateSessionId();
        
        SessionData sessionData = new SessionData();
        sessionData.setUsername(username);
        sessionData.setEncryptedPassword(encryptionService.encrypt(password));
        sessionData.setAuthenticated(true);
        sessionData.setCreatedAt(System.currentTimeMillis());
        sessionData.setExpiresAt(System.currentTimeMillis() + SESSION_TTL.toMillis());
        sessionData.setSwapStatus("Idle");
        
        try {
            String sessionJson = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(sessionData);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                "session:" + sessionId, 
                sessionJson, 
                SESSION_TTL
            );
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to create session", e);
        }
        
        return sessionId;
    }

    public SessionData getSecureSession(String sessionId) {
        try {
            String sessionJson = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("session:" + sessionId);
            if (sessionJson == null) {
                throw new SecurityException("Session expired or invalid");
            }
            
            SessionData sessionData = objectMapper.readValue(sessionJson, SessionData.class);
            
            if (System.currentTimeMillis() > sessionData.getExpiresAt()) {
                redisTemplate.delete("session:" + sessionId);
                throw new SecurityException("Session expired");
            }
            
            return sessionData;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to retrieve session", e);
        }
    }

    private String generateSessionId() {
        // Generate URL-safe random string
        byte[] bytes = new byte[32];
        ThreadLocalRandom.current().nextBytes(bytes);
        return java.util.Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(bytes);
    }
}
````

### 4. Selenium Service (Web automation)
````java
package com.ntu.addrop.service;

import com.ntu.addrop.util.WebDriverUtil;
import org.openqa.selenium.By;
import org.openqa.selenium.WebDriver;
import org.openqa.selenium.WebElement;
import org.openqa.selenium.support.ui.WebDriverWait;
import org.openqa.selenium.support.ui.ExpectedConditions;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.scheduling.annotation.Async;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.concurrent.CompletableFuture;

@Service
public class SeleniumService {

    @Autowired
    private WebDriverUtil webDriverUtil;
    
    @Autowired
    private SessionService sessionService;

    @Async
    public CompletableFuture<Void> performSwaps(
            String username, 
            String password, 
            String sessionId) {
        
        WebDriver driver = null;
        try {
            driver = webDriverUtil.getDriver();
            
            // Update status
            sessionService.updateSwapStatus(sessionId, "Processing", "Logging into NTU portal...");
            
            boolean loginSuccess = loginToPortal(driver, username, password, sessionId);
            if (!loginSuccess) {
                return CompletableFuture.completedFuture(null);
            }
            
            // Perform swap logic here...
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            sessionService.updateSwapStatus(sessionId, "Error", "An error occurred: " + e.getMessage());
        } finally {
            if (driver != null) {
                webDriverUtil.releaseDriver(driver);
            }
        }
        
        return CompletableFuture.completedFuture(null);
    }

    private boolean loginToPortal(WebDriver driver, String username, String password, String sessionId) {
        try {
            String url = "https://wish.wis.ntu.edu.sg/pls/webexe/ldap_login.login?w_url=https://wish.wis.ntu.edu.sg/pls/webexe/aus_stars_planner.main";
            driver.get(url);

            WebElement usernameField = driver.findElement(By.id("UID"));
            usernameField.sendKeys(username);
            
            WebElement okButton = driver.findElement(By.xpath("//input[@value='OK']"));
            okButton.click();

            WebDriverWait wait = new WebDriverWait(driver, Duration.ofSeconds(10));
            wait.until(ExpectedConditions.presenceOfElementLocated(By.id("PW")));

            WebElement passwordField = driver.findElement(By.id("PW"));
            passwordField.sendKeys(password);
            
            okButton = driver.findElement(By.xpath("//input[@value='OK']"));
            okButton.click();

            // Wait for successful login or handle errors
            return true;
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            sessionService.updateSwapStatus(sessionId, "Error", "Login failed: " + e.getMessage());
            return false;
        }
    }
}
````

````xml
	</properties>
	<dependencies>
		<!-- Spring Boot Starters -->
		<dependency>
			<groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
			<artifactId>spring-boot-starter-web</artifactId>
		</dependency>
		<dependency>
			<groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
			<artifactId>spring-boot-starter-data-redis</artifactId>
		</dependency>
````

````xml
			<groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
			<artifactId>spring-boot-starter-security</artifactId>
		</dependency>
		<dependency>
			<groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
			<artifactId>spring-boot-starter-validation</artifactId>
		</dependency>
		<dependency>
			<groupId>org.springframework.boot</groupId>
			<artifactId>spring-boot-devtools</artifactId>
			<scope>runtime</scope>
			<optional>true</optional>
		</dependency>

							<artifactId>spring-boot-devtools</artifactId>
						</exclude>
					</excludes>
				</configuration>
			</plugin>
		</plugins>
	</build>
</project>
````

### Step 7: Create Basic Controller Structure

````java
package com.ntu.addrop.controller;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.time.LocalDateTime;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
public class HealthController {

    @Autowired
    private RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;

    @GetMapping("/")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> root() {
        return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
            "message", "NTU Add-Drop Automator Java Backend API",
            "status", "running",
            "timestamp", LocalDateTime.now()
        ));
    }

    @GetMapping("/health")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> healthCheck() {
        try {
            // Test Redis connection
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set("health-check", "ok");
            String value = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("health-check");
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "status", "healthy",
                "redis", "connected",
                "redis-test", value,
                "timestamp", LocalDateTime.now()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(503).body(Map.of(
                "status", "unhealthy", 
                "redis", "disconnected: " + e.getMessage(),
                "timestamp", LocalDateTime.now()
            ));
        }
    }
}
````

### Step 8: Create Basic Configuration Classes

````java
package com.ntu.addrop.config;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.data.redis.connection.RedisConnectionFactory;
import org.springframework.data.redis.connection.RedisStandaloneConfiguration;
import org.springframework.data.redis.connection.jedis.JedisConnectionFactory;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.data.redis.serializer.StringRedisSerializer;

@Configuration
public class RedisConfig {

    @Value("${spring.redis.host}")
    private String redisHost;

    @Value("${spring.redis.port}")
    private int redisPort;

    @Bean
    public RedisConnectionFactory redisConnectionFactory() {
        RedisStandaloneConfiguration config = new RedisStandaloneConfiguration();
        config.setHostName(redisHost);
        config.setPort(redisPort);
        return new JedisConnectionFactory(config);
    }

    @Bean
    public RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate(RedisConnectionFactory connectionFactory) {
        RedisTemplate<String, String> template = new RedisTemplate<>();
        template.setConnectionFactory(connectionFactory);
        
        // Use String serializers for both keys and values
        template.setKeySerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setValueSerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setHashKeySerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setHashValueSerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        
        template.afterPropertiesSet();
        return template;
    }
}
````

````java
package com.ntu.addrop.config;

import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.web.cors.CorsConfiguration;
import org.springframework.web.cors.CorsConfigurationSource;
import org.springframework.web.cors.UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource;

import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.List;

@Configuration
public class CorsConfig {

    @Bean
    public CorsConfigurationSource corsConfigurationSource() {
        CorsConfiguration configuration = new CorsConfiguration();
        
        configuration.setAllowedOrigins(Arrays.asList(
            "http://localhost:3000",  // React dev server
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",  // Vercel frontend
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app"  // Vercel preview deployments
        ));
        
        configuration.setAllowedMethods(Arrays.asList("GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "OPTIONS"));
        configuration.setAllowedHeaders(List.of("*"));
        configuration.setAllowCredentials(true);
        
        UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource source = new UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource();
        source.registerCorsConfiguration("/api/**", configuration);
        
        return source;
    }
}
````

### Step 9: Disable Security Temporarily

````java
package com.ntu.addrop.config;

import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.security.config.annotation.web.builders.HttpSecurity;
import org.springframework.security.config.annotation.web.configuration.EnableWebSecurity;
import org.springframework.security.web.SecurityFilterChain;

@Configuration
@EnableWebSecurity
public class SecurityConfig {

    @Bean
    public SecurityFilterChain filterChain(HttpSecurity http) throws Exception {
        http
            .csrf(csrf -> csrf.disable())  // Disable CSRF for API
            .authorizeHttpRequests(auth -> auth
                .anyRequest().permitAll()  // Allow all requests for now
            );
        
        return http.build();
    }
}
````

### Create the `config` package and files:

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.config;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
import org.springframework.data.redis.connection.RedisConnectionFactory;
import org.springframework.data.redis.connection.RedisStandaloneConfiguration;
import org.springframework.data.redis.connection.jedis.JedisConnectionFactory;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.data.redis.serializer.StringRedisSerializer;

@Configuration
public class RedisConfig {

    @Value("${spring.redis.host}")
    private String redisHost;

    @Value("${spring.redis.port}")
    private int redisPort;

    @Bean
    public RedisConnectionFactory redisConnectionFactory() {
        RedisStandaloneConfiguration config = new RedisStandaloneConfiguration();
        config.setHostName(redisHost);
        config.setPort(redisPort);
        return new JedisConnectionFactory(config);
    }

    @Bean
    public RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate(RedisConnectionFactory connectionFactory) {
        RedisTemplate<String, String> template = new RedisTemplate<>();
        template.setConnectionFactory(connectionFactory);
        
        // Use String serializers for both keys and values
        template.setKeySerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setValueSerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setHashKeySerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        template.setHashValueSerializer(new StringRedisSerializer());
        
        template.afterPropertiesSet();
        return template;
    }
}
````

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.config;

import org.springframework.context.annotation.Bean;
import org.springframework.context.annotation.Configuration;
````
````java
import org.springframework.web.cors.CorsConfiguration;
import org.springframework.web.cors.CorsConfigurationSource;
import org.springframework.web.cors.UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource;

import java.util.Arrays;
import java.util.List;

@Configuration
public class CorsConfig {

    @Bean
    public CorsConfigurationSource corsConfigurationSource() {
        CorsConfiguration configuration = new CorsConfiguration();
        
        configuration.setAllowedOrigins(Arrays.asList(
            "http://localhost:3000",  // React dev server
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",  // Vercel frontend
            "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app"  // Vercel preview deployments
        ));
        
        configuration.setAllowedMethods(Arrays.asList("GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "OPTIONS"));
        configuration.setAllowedHeaders(List.of("*"));
        configuration.setAllowCredentials(true);
        
        UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource source = new UrlBasedCorsConfigurationSource();
        source.registerCorsConfiguration("/api/**", configuration);
        
        return source;
    }
}
````

## 5. Create the Health Controller

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.controller;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.GetMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RequestMapping;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.RestController;

import java.time.LocalDateTime;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
public class HealthController {

    @Autowired
    private RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;

    @GetMapping("/")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> root() {
        return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
            "message", "NTU Add-Drop Automator Java Backend API",
            "status", "running",
            "timestamp", LocalDateTime.now()
        ));
    }

    @GetMapping("/health")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> healthCheck() {
        try {
            // Test Redis connection
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set("health-check", "ok");
            String value = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("health-check");
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "status", "healthy",
                "redis", "connected",
                "redis-test", value,
                "timestamp", LocalDateTime.now()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(503).body(Map.of(
                "status", "unhealthy", 
                "redis", "disconnected: " + e.getMessage(),
                "timestamp", LocalDateTime.now()
            ));
        }
    }
}
````

## Issue 2: Redis Not Running

Yes, you're right! We've only configured Redis in Spring Boot, but **Redis server itself isn't running**. You have two options:

### Option A: Install Redis Locally (Recommended for Development)

**1. Download Redis for Windows:**
- Go to: https://github.com/tporadowski/redis/releases
- Download `Redis-x64-5.0.14.1.msi` (or latest version)
- Install it

```bash
# After installation, Redis should start automatically

# Test if Redis is running:
redis-cli ping
# Should return: PONG
```

**3. Verify Redis is working:**
```bash
# Test Redis connection
redis-cli
127.0.0.1:6379> set test "hello"
127.0.0.1:6379> get test
# Should return: "hello"
127.0.0.1:6379> exit
```

### Option B: Use Docker Redis (Alternative)

If you have Docker installed:

```bash
# Run Redis in Docker
docker run --name redis-dev -p 6379:6379 -d redis:alpine

# Check if it's running
docker ps
```

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.model;

import jakarta.validation.constraints.NotBlank;
import jakarta.validation.constraints.Size;

public class LoginRequest {
    
    @NotBlank(message = "Username is required")
    @Size(min = 1, max = 50, message = "Username must be between 1 and 50 characters")
    private String username;
    
    @NotBlank(message = "Password is required")
    @Size(min = 1, max = 100, message = "Password must be between 1 and 100 characters")
    private String password;
    
    // Constructors
    public LoginRequest() {}
    
    public LoginRequest(String username, String password) {
        this.username = username;
        this.password = password;
    }
    
    // Getters and Setters
    public String getUsername() {
        return username;
    }
    
    public void setUsername(String username) {
        this.username = username;
    }
    
    public String getPassword() {
        return password;
    }
    
    public void setPassword(String password) {
        this.password = password;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String toString() {
        return "LoginRequest{username='" + username + "'}"; // Don't log password!
    }
}
````

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.model;
````

### Step 2: Create Session Service

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.service;

import com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper;
import com.ntu.adddrop.model.SessionData;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.security.SecureRandom;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.Base64;
import java.util.List;

@Service
public class SessionService {

    @Autowired
    private RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private ObjectMapper objectMapper;

    private static final Duration SESSION_TTL = Duration.ofHours(2);
    private static final SecureRandom secureRandom = new SecureRandom();

    public String createSecureSession(String username, String encryptedPassword) {
        String sessionId = generateSessionId();
        
        SessionData sessionData = new SessionData();
        sessionData.setUsername(username);
        sessionData.setEncryptedPassword(encryptedPassword);
        sessionData.setAuthenticated(true);
        sessionData.setCreatedAt(System.currentTimeMillis());
        sessionData.setExpiresAt(System.currentTimeMillis() + SESSION_TTL.toMillis());
        sessionData.setSwapStatus("Idle");
        sessionData.setSwapMessage("Session created successfully");
        
        try {
            String sessionJson = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(sessionData);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                "session:" + sessionId, 
                sessionJson, 
                SESSION_TTL
            );
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to create session", e);
        }
        
        return sessionId;
    }

    public SessionData getSecureSession(String sessionId) {
        try {
            String sessionJson = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("session:" + sessionId);
            if (sessionJson == null) {
                throw new SecurityException("Session expired or invalid");
            }
            
            SessionData sessionData = objectMapper.readValue(sessionJson, SessionData.class);
            
            if (System.currentTimeMillis() > sessionData.getExpiresAt()) {
                redisTemplate.delete("session:" + sessionId);
                throw new SecurityException("Session expired");
            }
            
            return sessionData;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            if (e instanceof SecurityException) {
                throw e;
            }
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to retrieve session", e);
        }
    }

    public void updateSwapStatus(String sessionId, String status, String message, List<String> details) {
        try {
            SessionData sessionData = getSecureSession(sessionId);
            sessionData.setSwapStatus(status);
            sessionData.setSwapMessage(message);
            if (details != null) {
                sessionData.setSwapDetails(details);
            }
            
            String sessionJson = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(sessionData);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                "session:" + sessionId, 
                sessionJson, 
                SESSION_TTL
            );
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to update session status", e);
        }
    }

    public void cleanupSession(String sessionId) {
        redisTemplate.delete("session:" + sessionId);
    }

    private String generateSessionId() {
        byte[] bytes = new byte[32];
        secureRandom.nextBytes(bytes);
        return Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(bytes);
    }
}
````

### 1. **LoginRequest.java** ↔ FastAPI `LoginRequest` (Pydantic model)
````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.model;

import jakarta.validation.constraints.NotBlank;
import jakarta.validation.constraints.Size;
import jakarta.validation.constraints.Min;

public class LoginRequest {
    
    @NotBlank(message = "Username is required")
    @Size(min = 1, max = 50, message = "Username must be between 1 and 50 characters")
    private String username;
    
    @NotBlank(message = "Password is required")
    @Size(min = 1, max = 100, message = "Password must be between 1 and 100 characters")
    private String password;
    
    @Min(value = 1, message = "Number of modules must be at least 1")
    private int numModules;  // matches your FastAPI num_modules
    
    // Constructors
    public LoginRequest() {}
    
    public LoginRequest(String username, String password, int numModules) {
        this.username = username;
        this.password = password;
        this.numModules = numModules;
    }
    
    // Getters and Setters
    public String getUsername() {
        return username;
    }
    
    public void setUsername(String username) {
        this.username = username;
    }
    
    public String getPassword() {
        return password;
    }
    
    public void setPassword(String password) {
        this.password = password;
    }
    
    public int getNumModules() {
        return numModules;
    }
    
    public void setNumModules(int numModules) {
        this.numModules = numModules;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String toString() {
        return "LoginRequest{username='" + username + "', numModules=" + numModules + "}";
    }
}
````

### 2. **SwapRequest.java** ↔ FastAPI `submit-swap` form data
````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.model;

import jakarta.validation.constraints.NotBlank;
import jakarta.validation.constraints.NotEmpty;
import jakarta.validation.constraints.Size;
import java.util.List;

public class SwapRequest {
    
    private int numModules;
    
    @NotEmpty(message = "At least one module is required")
    private List<ModuleData> modules;
    
    // Nested class for module swap data - matches your FastAPI logic
    public static class ModuleData {
        @NotBlank(message = "Old index is required")
        @Size(max = 10, message = "Old index must not exceed 10 characters")
        private String oldIndex;  // matches your old_index
        
        @NotBlank(message = "New indexes are required")
        private String newIndexes; // comma-separated string, matches your new_indexes
        
        // Constructors
        public ModuleData() {}
        
        public ModuleData(String oldIndex, String newIndexes) {
            this.oldIndex = oldIndex;
            this.newIndexes = newIndexes;
        }
        
        // Getters and Setters
        public String getOldIndex() {
            return oldIndex;
        }
        
        public void setOldIndex(String oldIndex) {
            this.oldIndex = oldIndex;
        }
        
        public String getNewIndexes() {
            return newIndexes;
        }
        
        public void setNewIndexes(String newIndexes) {
            this.newIndexes = newIndexes;
        }
        
        // Helper method to parse comma-separated indexes (like your FastAPI logic)
        public List<String> getNewIndexesList() {
            return List.of(newIndexes.split(","))
                      .stream()
                      .map(String::trim)
                      .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
                      .toList();
        }
        
        @Override
        public String toString() {
            return "ModuleData{oldIndex='" + oldIndex + "', newIndexes='" + newIndexes + "'}";
        }
    }
    
    // Constructors
    public SwapRequest() {}
    
    public SwapRequest(int numModules, List<ModuleData> modules) {
        this.numModules = numModules;
        this.modules = modules;
    }
    
    // Getters and Setters
    public int getNumModules() {
        return numModules;
    }
    
    public void setNumModules(int numModules) {
        this.numModules = numModules;
    }
    
    public List<ModuleData> getModules() {
        return modules;
    }
    
    public void setModules(List<ModuleData> modules) {
        this.modules = modules;
    }
    
    @Override
    public String toString() {
        return "SwapRequest{numModules=" + numModules + ", modules=" + modules + "}";
    }
}
````

### 3. **SessionData.java** ↔ FastAPI session data structure
````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.model;

import com.fasterxml.jackson.annotation.JsonProperty;
import java.util.List;

// Exactly matches your FastAPI session_data structure
public class SessionData {
    
    private String username;
    
    @JsonProperty("encrypted_password")
    private String encryptedPassword;
    
    private boolean authenticated;
    
    @JsonProperty("created_at")
    private long createdAt;
    
    @JsonProperty("expires_at") 
    private long expiresAt;
    
    // Swap status fields - matches your FastAPI exactly
    @JsonProperty("swap_status")
    private String swapStatus; // "Idle", "Processing", "Completed", "Error", "Stopped", "Timed Out"
    
    @JsonProperty("swap_message")
    private String swapMessage;
    
    @JsonProperty("swap_started_at")
    private Long swapStartedAt;
    
    // Module data - matches your FastAPI modules structure
    private List<ModuleStatus> modules;
    
    // Nested class for module status - matches your FastAPI module structure
    public static class ModuleStatus {
        @JsonProperty("old_index")
        private String oldIndex;
        
        @JsonProperty("new_indexes")
        private List<String> newIndexes;
        
        private boolean swapped;
        
        private String message;
        
        // Constructors
        public ModuleStatus() {}

            this.newIndexes = newIndexes;
````

### 4. **SessionService.java** ↔ FastAPI session management functions
````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.service;

import com.fasterxml.jackson.databind.ObjectMapper;
import com.ntu.adddrop.model.SessionData;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.data.redis.core.RedisTemplate;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import java.security.SecureRandom;
import java.time.Duration;
import java.util.Base64;
import java.util.List;

@Service
public class SessionService {

    @Autowired
    private RedisTemplate<String, String> redisTemplate;
    
    @Autowired
    private ObjectMapper objectMapper;

    private static final Duration SESSION_TTL = Duration.ofHours(2); // matches your 7200 seconds
    private static final SecureRandom secureRandom = new SecureRandom();

    // Matches your create_secure_session function
    public String createSecureSession(String username, String encryptedPassword) {
        String sessionId = generateSessionId(); // matches your secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
        
        SessionData sessionData = new SessionData();
        sessionData.setUsername(username);
        sessionData.setEncryptedPassword(encryptedPassword);
        sessionData.setAuthenticated(true);
        sessionData.setCreatedAt(System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000); // matches your time.time()
        sessionData.setExpiresAt(System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000 + 7200); // matches your 2 hours
        sessionData.setSwapStatus("Idle"); // matches your initial status
        sessionData.setSwapMessage(null);
        sessionData.setSwapStartedAt(null);
        
        try {
            String sessionJson = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(sessionData);
            redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                "session:" + sessionId,  // matches your f"session:{session_id}"
                sessionJson, 
                SESSION_TTL
            );
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to create session", e);
        }
        
        return sessionId;
    }

    // Matches your get_secure_session function
    public SessionData getSecureSession(String sessionId) {
        try {
            String sessionJson = redisTemplate.opsForValue().get("session:" + sessionId);
            if (sessionJson == null) {
                throw new SecurityException("Session expired or invalid");
            }
            
            SessionData sessionData = objectMapper.readValue(sessionJson, SessionData.class);
            
            // Matches your expiration check
            if (System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000 > sessionData.getExpiresAt()) {
                redisTemplate.delete("session:" + sessionId);
                throw new SecurityException("Session expired");
            }
            
            return sessionData;
        } catch (Exception e) {
            if (e instanceof SecurityException) {
                throw e;
            }
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to retrieve session", e);
        }
    }

    // Matches your update_session_data function
    public void updateSessionData(String sessionId, SessionData updates) {
        try {
            SessionData sessionData = getSecureSession(sessionId);
            
            // Update fields that are not null
            if (updates.getSwapStatus() != null) {
                sessionData.setSwapStatus(updates.getSwapStatus());
            }
            if (updates.getSwapMessage() != null) {
                sessionData.setSwapMessage(updates.getSwapMessage());
            }
            if (updates.getSwapStartedAt() != null) {
                sessionData.setSwapStartedAt(updates.getSwapStartedAt());
            }
            if (updates.getModules() != null) {
                sessionData.setModules(updates.getModules());
            }
            
            String sessionJson = objectMapper.writeValueAsString(sessionData);
            
            // Maintain TTL like your FastAPI version
            Long ttl = redisTemplate.getExpire("session:" + sessionId);
            if (ttl != null && ttl > 0) {
                redisTemplate.opsForValue().set(
                    "session:" + sessionId, 
                    sessionJson, 
                    Duration.ofSeconds(ttl)
                );
            }
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to update session", e);
        }
    }

    // Matches your update_overall_swap_status function
    public void updateOverallSwapStatus(String sessionId, String status, String message) {
        SessionData updates = new SessionData();
        updates.setSwapStatus(status);
        updates.setSwapMessage(message);
        updateSessionData(sessionId, updates);
    }

    // Clean up session - matches your Redis delete operations
    public void cleanupSession(String sessionId) {
        redisTemplate.delete("session:" + sessionId);
    }

    // Matches your secrets.token_urlsafe(32)
    private String generateSessionId() {
        byte[] bytes = new byte[32];
        secureRandom.nextBytes(bytes);
        return Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(bytes);
    }
}
````

### 1. EncryptionService.java (Port your FastAPI encryption logic)

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.security;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import javax.crypto.Cipher;
import javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.security.MessageDigest;
import java.util.Base64;

@Service
public class EncryptionService {

    @Value("${app.encryption.key}")
    private String encryptionKey;

    private static final String ALGORITHM = "AES";
    private static final String TRANSFORMATION = "AES/ECB/PKCS5Padding";

    /**
     * Encrypts plaintext using AES encryption
     * Matches your FastAPI encrypt_credentials function
     */
    public String encrypt(String plainText) {
        try {
            // Create a key from the encryption key (hash it to ensure 32 bytes)
            SecretKeySpec secretKey = createSecretKey(encryptionKey);
            
            // Initialize cipher for encryption
            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);
            cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, secretKey);
            
            // Encrypt the plaintext
            byte[] encryptedBytes = cipher.doFinal(plainText.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
            
            // Return Base64 encoded result (matches your FastAPI base64 encoding)
            return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(encryptedBytes);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to encrypt data", e);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Decrypts encrypted text using AES decryption
     * Matches your FastAPI decrypt_credentials function
     */
    public String decrypt(String encryptedText) {
        try {
            // Create a key from the encryption key
            SecretKeySpec secretKey = createSecretKey(encryptionKey);
            
            // Initialize cipher for decryption
            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);
            cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, secretKey);
            
            // Decode Base64 and decrypt
            byte[] encryptedBytes = Base64.getDecoder().decode(encryptedText);
            byte[] decryptedBytes = cipher.doFinal(encryptedBytes);
            
            // Return decrypted string
            return new String(decryptedBytes, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to decrypt data", e);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Creates a proper AES key from the provided key string
     * Matches your FastAPI key derivation
     */
    private SecretKeySpec createSecretKey(String key) {
        try {
            // Hash the key to ensure it's exactly 32 bytes (256 bits) for AES-256
            MessageDigest sha = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256");
            byte[] keyBytes = sha.digest(key.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
            return new SecretKeySpec(keyBytes, ALGORITHM);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to create encryption key", e);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Utility method to validate if text is likely encrypted
     */
    public boolean isEncrypted(String text) {
        try {
            // Try to decode as Base64 - if it fails, probably not encrypted
            Base64.getDecoder().decode(text);
            return true;
        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {
            return false;
        }
    }
}
````

### 2. AuthController.java (Port your FastAPI `/api/login` endpoint)

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.controller;

import com.ntu.adddrop.model.LoginRequest;
import com.ntu.adddrop.model.SessionData;
import com.ntu.adddrop.security.EncryptionService;
import com.ntu.adddrop.service.SessionService;
import jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;
import jakarta.validation.Valid;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
@CrossOrigin(origins = {
    "http://localhost:3000", 
    "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
    "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app"
})
public class AuthController {

    @Autowired
    private SessionService sessionService;
    
    @Autowired
    private EncryptionService encryptionService;

    /**
     * Login endpoint - matches your FastAPI /api/login
     */
    @PostMapping("/login") 
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> login(
            @Valid @RequestBody LoginRequest loginRequest,
            HttpServletRequest request) {
        
        try {
            // Encrypt the password (matches your FastAPI encrypt_credentials)
            String encryptedPassword = encryptionService.encrypt(loginRequest.getPassword());
            
            // Create secure session (matches your FastAPI create_secure_session)
            String sessionId = sessionService.createSecureSession(
                loginRequest.getUsername(), 
                encryptedPassword
            );
            
            // Store session in HTTP session (matches your FastAPI)
            request.getSession().setAttribute("session_id", sessionId);
            request.getSession().setAttribute("authenticated", true);
            request.getSession().setAttribute("num_modules", loginRequest.getNumModules());
            
            // Return success response (matches your FastAPI response)
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "message", "Login successful",
                "session_id", sessionId,
                "num_modules", loginRequest.getNumModules()
            ));
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            // Return error response (matches your FastAPI error handling)
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Login failed: " + e.getMessage()
            ));
        }
    }

    /**
     * Logout endpoint - matches your FastAPI /api/logout/{session_id}
     */
    @PostMapping("/logout/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> logout(
            @PathVariable String sessionId,
            HttpServletRequest request) {
        
        try {
            // Clean up session from Redis
            sessionService.cleanupSession(sessionId);
            
            // Invalidate HTTP session
            request.getSession().invalidate();
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "message", "Logged out successfully"
            ));
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Logout failed: " + e.getMessage()
            ));
        }
    }

    /**
     * Check session status - additional endpoint for frontend
     */
    @GetMapping("/session-status/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getSessionStatus(@PathVariable String sessionId) {
        try {
            SessionData sessionData = sessionService.getSecureSession(sessionId);
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "authenticated", sessionData.isAuthenticated(),
                "username", sessionData.getUsername(),
                "swap_status", sessionData.getSwapStatus(),
                "created_at", sessionData.getCreatedAt(),
                "expires_at", sessionData.getExpiresAt()
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Failed to get session status"
            ));
        }
    }
}
````

### 3. SwapController.java (Port your FastAPI swap endpoints)

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.controller;

import com.ntu.adddrop.model.SessionData;
import com.ntu.adddrop.model.SwapRequest;
import com.ntu.adddrop.service.SessionService;
import jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;
import jakarta.validation.Valid;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
@CrossOrigin(origins = {
    "http://localhost:3000", 
    "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
    "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app"
})
public class SwapController {

    @Autowired
    private SessionService sessionService;

    /**
     * Submit swap request - matches your FastAPI /api/submit-swap
     */
    @PostMapping("/submit-swap")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> submitSwap(
            @Valid @RequestBody SwapRequest swapRequest,
            HttpServletRequest request) {
        
        try {
            // Get session ID from HTTP session (matches your FastAPI)
            String sessionId = (String) request.getSession().getAttribute("session_id");
            if (sessionId == null) {
                return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                    "success", false,
                    "message", "No active session found"
                ));
            }

            // Verify session exists and is valid
            SessionData sessionData = sessionService.getSecureSession(sessionId);
            
            // Convert SwapRequest to SessionData module format
            List<SessionData.ModuleStatus> modules = new ArrayList<>();
            for (SwapRequest.ModuleData moduleData : swapRequest.getModules()) {
                SessionData.ModuleStatus moduleStatus = new SessionData.ModuleStatus();
                moduleStatus.setOldIndex(moduleData.getOldIndex());
                moduleStatus.setNewIndexes(moduleData.getNewIndexesList());  // Convert comma-separated to list
                moduleStatus.setSwapped(false);
                moduleStatus.setMessage("Pending");
                modules.add(moduleStatus);
            }
            
            // Update session with module data and start swap
            SessionData updates = new SessionData();
            updates.setModules(modules);
            updates.setSwapStatus("Processing");
            updates.setSwapMessage("Swap request submitted, starting automation...");
            updates.setSwapStartedAt(System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000);
            
            sessionService.updateSessionData(sessionId, updates);
            
            // TODO: Start async Selenium automation (Phase 3)
            // For now, just return success
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "message", "Swap request submitted successfully",
                "session_id", sessionId
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Failed to submit swap request: " + e.getMessage()
            ));
        }
    }

    /**
     * Get swap status - matches your FastAPI /api/swap-status/{session_id}
     */
    @GetMapping("/swap-status/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getSwapStatus(@PathVariable String sessionId) {
        try {
            SessionData sessionData = sessionService.getSecureSession(sessionId);
            
            // Return status in format matching your FastAPI response
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "status", sessionData.getSwapStatus(),
                "message", sessionData.getSwapMessage() != null ? sessionData.getSwapMessage() : "",
                "started_at", sessionData.getSwapStartedAt(),
                "details", sessionData.getModules() != null ? sessionData.getModules() : new ArrayList<>()
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "status", "Error",
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "status", "Error", 
                "message", "Failed to get swap status"
            ));
        }
    }

    /**
     * Stop swap process - matches your FastAPI /api/stop-swap/{session_id}
     */
    @PostMapping("/stop-swap/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> stopSwap(@PathVariable String sessionId) {
        try {
            // Update session to stopped status
            sessionService.updateOverallSwapStatus(sessionId, "Stopped", "Swap process stopped by user");
            
            // TODO: Stop async Selenium process (Phase 3)
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "message", "Swap process stopped successfully"
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Failed to stop swap process"
            ));
        }
    }
}
````

GitHub Copilot: Excellent analysis! You've identified some important design issues. Let me address your questions and provide fixes:

## Your Questions Answered:

### 1. **SessionData Constructor**: ✅ Already exists
Your [`backend-java/src/main/java/com/ntu/adddrop/model/SessionData.java`]SessionData.java ) already has a default constructor: `public SessionData() {}` - this is sufficient for Spring Boot and Jackson.

### 2. **SwapRequest vs SessionData Conflict**: ✅ You're absolutely RIGHT!
SwapRequest is indeed redundant and adds unnecessary overhead. SessionData is more comprehensive and contains everything we need. Let's remove SwapRequest entirely.

### 3. **Constructor Patterns**: Different purposes
- **SessionService**: No constructor needed - Spring manages it as a singleton service
- **LoginRequest**: Needs constructors for data transfer from frontend
- **SessionData**: Default constructor is sufficient for Jackson JSON serialization

### Step 2: Update SwapController.java to use SessionData directly

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.controller;

import com.ntu.adddrop.model.SessionData;
import com.ntu.adddrop.service.SessionService;
import jakarta.servlet.http.HttpServletRequest;
import jakarta.validation.Valid;
import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Autowired;
import org.springframework.http.ResponseEntity;
import org.springframework.web.bind.annotation.*;

import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;
import java.util.Map;

@RestController
@RequestMapping("/api")
@CrossOrigin(origins = {
    "http://localhost:3000", 
    "https://ntu-add-drop-automator.vercel.app",
    "https://ntu-add-drop-automator-*.vercel.app"
})
public class SwapController {

    @Autowired
    private SessionService sessionService;

    /**
     * Submit swap request - now accepts module data directly in request body
     * Matches your FastAPI /api/submit-swap but simplified
     */
    @PostMapping("/submit-swap")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> submitSwap(
            @RequestBody Map<String, Object> swapData,  // ✅ Simple map instead of SwapRequest
            HttpServletRequest request) {
        
        try {
            // Get session ID from HTTP session
            String sessionId = (String) request.getSession().getAttribute("session_id");
            if (sessionId == null) {
                return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                    "success", false,
                    "message", "No active session found"
                ));
            }

            // Verify session exists and is valid
            SessionData sessionData = sessionService.getSecureSession(sessionId);
            
            // Extract module data from request (matches your FastAPI form data structure)
            @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
            List<Map<String, Object>> moduleDataList = (List<Map<String, Object>>) swapData.get("modules");
            
            if (moduleDataList == null || moduleDataList.isEmpty()) {
                return ResponseEntity.status(400).body(Map.of(
                    "success", false,
                    "message", "No module data provided"
                ));
            }
            
            // Convert to SessionData.ModuleStatus objects directly
            List<SessionData.ModuleStatus> modules = new ArrayList<>();
            for (Map<String, Object> moduleMap : moduleDataList) {
                SessionData.ModuleStatus moduleStatus = new SessionData.ModuleStatus();
                moduleStatus.setOldIndex((String) moduleMap.get("old_index"));
                
                // Handle new_indexes - could be string (comma-separated) or list
                Object newIndexesObj = moduleMap.get("new_indexes");
                List<String> newIndexes;
                if (newIndexesObj instanceof String) {
                    // Split comma-separated string
                    String newIndexesStr = (String) newIndexesObj;
                    newIndexes = List.of(newIndexesStr.split(","))
                                    .stream()
                                    .map(String::trim)
                                    .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
                                    .toList();
                } else {
                    // Already a list
                    @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
                    List<String> indexList = (List<String>) newIndexesObj;
                    newIndexes = indexList;
                }
                
                moduleStatus.setNewIndexes(newIndexes);
                moduleStatus.setSwapped(false);
                moduleStatus.setMessage("Pending");
                modules.add(moduleStatus);
            }
            
            // Update session with module data and start swap
            SessionData updates = new SessionData();
            updates.setModules(modules);
            updates.setSwapStatus("Processing");
            updates.setSwapMessage("Swap request submitted, starting automation...");
            updates.setSwapStartedAt(System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000);
            
            sessionService.updateSessionData(sessionId, updates);
            
            // TODO: Start async Selenium automation (Phase 3)
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "message", "Swap request submitted successfully",
                "session_id", sessionId
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Failed to submit swap request: " + e.getMessage()
            ));
        }
    }

    /**
     * Get swap status - matches your FastAPI /api/swap-status/{session_id}
     */
    @GetMapping("/swap-status/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> getSwapStatus(@PathVariable String sessionId) {
        try {
            SessionData sessionData = sessionService.getSecureSession(sessionId);
            
            // Return status in format matching your FastAPI response
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "status", sessionData.getSwapStatus() != null ? sessionData.getSwapStatus() : "Idle",
                "message", sessionData.getSwapMessage() != null ? sessionData.getSwapMessage() : "",
                "started_at", sessionData.getSwapStartedAt(),
                "details", sessionData.getModules() != null ? sessionData.getModules() : new ArrayList<>()
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "status", "Error",
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "status", "Error", 
                "message", "Failed to get swap status"
            ));
        }
    }

    /**
     * Stop swap process - matches your FastAPI /api/stop-swap/{session_id}
     */
    @PostMapping("/stop-swap/{sessionId}")
    public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> stopSwap(@PathVariable String sessionId) {
        try {
            // Update session to stopped status
            sessionService.updateOverallSwapStatus(sessionId, "Stopped", "Swap process stopped by user");
            
            // TODO: Stop async Selenium process (Phase 3)
            
            return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
                "success", true,
                "message", "Swap process stopped successfully"
            ));
            
        } catch (SecurityException e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", e.getMessage()
            ));
        } catch (Exception e) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "Failed to stop swap process"
            ));
        }
    }
}
````

### Step 3: Create the Missing EncryptionService.java

````java
package com.ntu.adddrop.security;

import org.springframework.beans.factory.annotation.Value;
import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;

import javax.crypto.Cipher;
import javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;
import java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;
import java.security.MessageDigest;
import java.util.Base64;

@Service
public class EncryptionService {

    @Value("${app.encryption.key}")
    private String encryptionKey;

    private static final String ALGORITHM = "AES";
    private static final String TRANSFORMATION = "AES/ECB/PKCS5Padding";

    /**
     * Encrypts plaintext using AES encryption
     * Matches your FastAPI encrypt_credentials function
     */
    public String encrypt(String plainText) {
        try {
            SecretKeySpec secretKey = createSecretKey(encryptionKey);
            
            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);
            cipher.init(Cipher.ENCRYPT_MODE, secretKey);
            
            byte[] encryptedBytes = cipher.doFinal(plainText.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
            
            return Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(encryptedBytes);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to encrypt data", e);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Decrypts encrypted text using AES decryption
     * Matches your FastAPI decrypt_credentials function
     */
    public String decrypt(String encryptedText) {
        try {
            SecretKeySpec secretKey = createSecretKey(encryptionKey);
            
            Cipher cipher = Cipher.getInstance(TRANSFORMATION);
            cipher.init(Cipher.DECRYPT_MODE, secretKey);
            
            byte[] encryptedBytes = Base64.getDecoder().decode(encryptedText);
            byte[] decryptedBytes = cipher.doFinal(encryptedBytes);
            
            return new String(decryptedBytes, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to decrypt data", e);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Creates a proper AES key from the provided key string
     */
    private SecretKeySpec createSecretKey(String key) {
        try {
            MessageDigest sha = MessageDigest.getInstance("SHA-256");
            byte[] keyBytes = sha.digest(key.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
            return new SecretKeySpec(keyBytes, ALGORITHM);
            
        } catch (Exception e) {
            throw new RuntimeException("Failed to create encryption key", e);
        }
    }

    /**
     * Utility method to validate if text is likely encrypted
     */
    public boolean isEncrypted(String text) {
        try {
            Base64.getDecoder().decode(text);
            return true;
        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {
            return false;
        }
    }
}
````

GitHub Copilot: Great questions! Let me clarify the Java constructor and class fundamentals:

## Understanding Java Constructors & Class Variables

### 1. **Class Variables (Fields) ARE Defined**

Looking at your [`backend-java/src/main/java/com/ntu/adddrop/model/SessionData.java`]SessionData.java ), the variables **ARE** defined at the top of the class:

```java
public class SessionData {
    
    private String username;           // ✅ DEFINED HERE
    private String encryptedPassword;  // ✅ DEFINED HERE
    private boolean authenticated;     // ✅ DEFINED HERE
    private long createdAt;           // ✅ DEFINED HERE
    private long expiresAt;           // ✅ DEFINED HERE
    // ... more fields
    
    // Constructor comes AFTER field declarations
    public SessionData() {}
    
    // Getters/setters reference the fields defined above
    public void setUsername(String username) {
        this.username = username;  // ✅ References the field defined at top
    }
}
```

### 2. **Why Empty Constructor vs Parameterized Constructor?**

**Empty Constructor `public SessionData() {}`:**
- **Purpose**: Allows creating an "empty" object that you fill later
- **Used by**: Spring Boot, Jackson JSON parsing, database frameworks
- **Example**: 
```java
SessionData data = new SessionData();  // Create empty object
data.setUsername("john");              // Fill it later
data.setAuthenticated(true);
```

**Parameterized Constructor in ModuleStatus:**
- **Purpose**: Convenience - create object with all values at once
- **Example**:
```java
// Option 1: Use parameterized constructor
ModuleStatus status = new ModuleStatus("12345", Arrays.asList("67890"), false, "Pending");

// Option 2: Use empty constructor + setters
ModuleStatus status = new ModuleStatus();
status.setOldIndex("12345");
status.setNewIndexes(Arrays.asList("67890"));
status.setSwapped(false);
status.setMessage("Pending");
```

### 3. **Why Both Constructors in ModuleStatus?**
- **Flexibility**: Allows both creation patterns
- **Framework Compatibility**: Empty constructor for Spring/Jackson
- **Developer Convenience**: Parameterized constructor for quick object creation

GitHub Copilot: Excellent question! You're absolutely right to think about efficiency. Let me explain why we use the "create empty + set individually" pattern here instead of the parameterized constructor.

## Why Not Use Parameterized Constructor Here?

You're correct that this would be faster and cleaner:

```java
// This WOULD be better IF we had all the data ready:
SessionData.ModuleStatus moduleStatus = new SessionData.ModuleStatus(
    oldIndex, 
    newIndexes, 
    false, 
    "Pending"
);
```

## The Problem: **Data Processing Pipeline**

But look at what's happening in your code:

````java
for (Map<String, Object> moduleMap : moduleDataList) {
    // Step 1: Get oldIndex (simple)
    String oldIndex = (String) moduleMap.get("oldIndex");
    
    // Step 2: Process newIndexes (COMPLEX - needs conditional logic)
    Object newIndexesObj = moduleMap.get("new_indexes");
    List<String> newIndexes;
    if (newIndexesObj instanceof String) {
        // Complex string processing
        String newIndexesStr = (String) newIndexesObj;
        newIndexes = List.of(newIndexesStr.split(","))
                        .stream()
                        .map(String::trim)
                        .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
                        .toList();
    } else {
        // Type casting
        @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
        List<String> indexList = (List<String>) newIndexesObj;
        newIndexes = indexList;
    }
    
    // Step 3: Now we can create the object
    // BUT we needed all that processing first!
}
````

## The Issue: **Sequential Data Processing**

We **cannot** use the parameterized constructor until **after** we've processed the `newIndexes` logic. We need to:

1. **Extract** `oldIndex` 
2. **Process & Transform** `newIndexes` (complex logic)
3. **Then** create the object

## Better Approach: Extract Processing Logic

Here's how we can make it cleaner **and** use the parameterized constructor:

````java
// Convert to SessionData.ModuleStatus objects directly from Form
List<SessionData.ModuleStatus> modules = new ArrayList<>();
for (Map<String, Object> moduleMap : moduleDataList) {
    
    // Extract and process data first
    String oldIndex = (String) moduleMap.get("oldIndex");
    List<String> newIndexes = processNewIndexes(moduleMap.get("new_indexes"));
    
    // Now create object with parameterized constructor
    SessionData.ModuleStatus moduleStatus = new SessionData.ModuleStatus(
        oldIndex, 
        newIndexes, 
        false, 
        "Pending"
    );
    
    modules.add(moduleStatus);
}

// Helper method to clean up the logic
private List<String> processNewIndexes(Object newIndexesObj) {
    if (newIndexesObj instanceof String) {
        String newIndexesStr = (String) newIndexesObj;
        return List.of(newIndexesStr.split(","))
                   .stream()
                   .map(String::trim)
                   .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
                   .toList();
    } else {
        @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
        List<String> indexList = (List<String>) newIndexesObj;
        return indexList;
    }
}
````

## Performance Analysis:

### Current Approach (Individual Setters):
```java
ModuleStatus obj = new ModuleStatus();  // 1 object creation
obj.setOldIndex(value);                 // 1 method call
obj.setNewIndexes(value);               // 1 method call  
obj.setSwapped(false);                  // 1 method call
obj.setMessage("Pending");              // 1 method call
// Total: 1 constructor + 4 setter calls
```

### Parameterized Constructor Approach:
```java
ModuleStatus obj = new ModuleStatus(oldIndex, newIndexes, false, "Pending");
// Total: 1 constructor call
```

**Performance difference**: Minimal in practice (nanoseconds), but cleaner code.

## Updated SwapController.java with Better Approach:

````java
@PostMapping("/submit-swap")
public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> submitSwap(
    @RequestBody Map<String, Object> swapData,
    HttpServletRequest request) {
    
    try {
        // Get session ID from HTTP session
        String sessionId = (String) request.getSession().getAttribute("session_id");
        if (sessionId == null) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "No active session found"
            ));
        }

        // Extract module data from request
        @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
        List<Map<String, Object>> moduleDataList = (List<Map<String, Object>>) swapData.get("modules");

        if (moduleDataList == null || moduleDataList.isEmpty()) {
            return ResponseEntity.status(400).body(Map.of(
                "success", false,
                "message", "No module data provided"
            ));
        }

        // Convert to SessionData.ModuleStatus objects - CLEANER APPROACH
        List<SessionData.ModuleStatus> modules = new ArrayList<>();
        for (Map<String, Object> moduleMap : moduleDataList) {
            
            // Extract and process data first
            String oldIndex = (String) moduleMap.get("oldIndex");
            List<String> newIndexes = processNewIndexes(moduleMap.get("new_indexes"));
            
            // Create object with parameterized constructor (faster & cleaner)
            SessionData.ModuleStatus moduleStatus = new SessionData.ModuleStatus(
                oldIndex, 
                newIndexes, 
                false, 
                "Pending"
            );
            
            modules.add(moduleStatus);
        }

        // Update session with module data and start swap
        SessionData updates = new SessionData();
        updates.setModules(modules);
        updates.setSwapStatus("Processing");
        updates.setSwapMessage("Swap request submitted, starting automation...");
        updates.setSwapStartedAt(System.currentTimeMillis() / 1000);

        sessionService.updateSessionData(sessionId, updates);

        return ResponseEntity.ok(Map.of(
            "success", true,
            "message", "Swap request submitted successfully",
            "session_id", sessionId
        ));
        
    } catch (SecurityException e) {
        return ResponseEntity.status(401).body(Map.of(
            "success", false,
            "message", e.getMessage()
        ));
    } catch (Exception e) {
        return ResponseEntity.status(500).body(Map.of(
            "success", false,
            "message", "Failed to submit swap request: " + e.getMessage()
        ));
    }
}

// Helper method for cleaner code organization
private List<String> processNewIndexes(Object newIndexesObj) {
    if (newIndexesObj instanceof String) {
        String newIndexesStr = (String) newIndexesObj;
        return List.of(newIndexesStr.split(","))
                   .stream()
                   .map(String::trim)
                   .filter(s -> !s.isEmpty())
                   .toList();
    } else {
        @SuppressWarnings("unchecked")
        List<String> indexList = (List<String>) newIndexesObj;
        return indexList;
    }
}
````

## Summary:

✅ **You're absolutely right** - parameterized constructor is faster and cleaner  
✅ **The reason we used setters** - we needed to process data first  
✅ **Better solution** - Extract processing logic, then use parameterized constructor  
✅ **Performance impact** - Minimal, but code clarity is much better  

**The key principle: Process data first, then create objects cleanly!** 🚀

GitHub Copilot: Great analysis! Let me address your questions:

## Your Questions Answered:

### 1. **Testing POST Requests** 
✅ **Yes, use Postman or similar tools** - browsers only do GET requests by default

### 2. **CORS Configuration Confusion**
The CORS origins in [`backend-java/src/main/java/com/ntu/adddrop/controller/AuthController.java`]AuthController.java ) are **correct**:
- `localhost:3000` = Your **React frontend** (different port)
- `localhost:8080` = Your **Spring Boot backend** (this port)

**CORS allows your React app (port 3000) to call your Spring Boot API (port 8080)**

### 3. **The Error Explained**
The error "Request method 'GET' is not supported" happens because:
- Browser does GET request to `/api/login`
- Your endpoint only accepts POST requests: `@PostMapping("/login")`
- **This is normal behavior!**

## Let's Test Your Setup Properly:

### **Method 1: Using curl (Command Line)**

```bash
# Test POST /api/login
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/login \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "username": "testuser",
    "password": "testpass",
    "numModules": 2
  }'
```

### **Method 2: Using Postman (Recommended)**

1. **Download Postman** (if you don't have it)
2. **Create new POST request**:
   - Method: `POST`
   - URL: `http://localhost:8080/api/login`
   - Headers: `Content-Type: application/json`
   - Body (raw JSON):
   ```json
   {
     "username": "testuser",
     "password": "testpass",  
     "numModules": 2
   }
   ```

josshhz11: hmm but from this POST /api/login endpoint, how do i know that the fields to pass in are "username", "password, "numModules"?

{
  "username": "testuser",
  "password": "testpass",  
  "numModules": 2
}

GitHub Copilot: Great question! Let me show you how to determine the expected JSON fields for any endpoint.

## How to Find Expected JSON Fields:

### **Method 1: Look at the Request Model Class**

Your [`backend-java/src/main/java/com/ntu/adddrop/controller/AuthController.java`]AuthController.java ) uses:

```java
@PostMapping("/login")
public ResponseEntity<Map<String, Object>> login(
    @Valid @RequestBody LoginRequest loginRequest,  // ← This tells you!
    HttpServletRequest request) {
```

The `@RequestBody LoginRequest` means it expects a `LoginRequest` object.

### **Method 2: Check the LoginRequest Class**

Looking at `LoginRequest.java`:

```java
public class LoginRequest {
    private String username;     // ← JSON field: "username"
    private String password;     // ← JSON field: "password" 
    private int numModules;      // ← JSON field: "numModules"
    
    // Getters and setters...
}
```

**The JSON field names match the Java field names exactly!**

### **Method 3: Spring Boot Magic** ✨

Spring Boot automatically maps JSON fields to Java fields using **Jackson**:
- Java field `username` ↔ JSON field `"username"`
- Java field `password` ↔ JSON field `"password"`
- Java field `numModules` ↔ JSON field `"numModules"`

## Alternative JSON Field Names

If you want different JSON field names, you can use `@JsonProperty`:

```java
public class LoginRequest {
    @JsonProperty("user_name")    // ← JSON would use "user_name"
    private String username;      // ← Java still uses "username"
    
    @JsonProperty("pass")         // ← JSON would use "pass"
    private String password;      // ← Java still uses "password"
    
    @JsonProperty("num_modules")  // ← JSON would use "num_modules"
    private int numModules;       // ← Java still uses "numModules"
}
```

## Quick Reference Guide:

### **For Any POST/PUT Endpoint:**

1. **Find the controller method**
2. **Look for `@RequestBody SomeClass`**
3. **Check that class's fields**
4. **Use those field names in your JSON**

### **Example Pattern:**

```java
// Controller method
@PostMapping("/some-endpoint")
public ResponseEntity<?> someMethod(@RequestBody MyRequest request) {
    // ...
}

// MyRequest class
public class MyRequest {
    private String fieldOne;      // ← JSON: "fieldOne"
    private int fieldTwo;         // ← JSON: "fieldTwo"
    private boolean fieldThree;   // ← JSON: "fieldThree"
}

// Expected JSON
{
    "fieldOne": "some value",
    "fieldTwo": 123,
    "fieldThree": true
}
```

## Testing Your Login Endpoint:

### **Correct JSON for `/api/login`:**

```json
{
  "username": "testuser",
  "password": "testpass",
  "numModules": 2
}
```

### **Postman Setup:**
1. **Method:** `POST`
2. **URL:** `http://localhost:8080/api/login`
3. **Headers:** `Content-Type: application/json`
4. **Body (raw JSON):** Use the JSON above

### **curl Command:**
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/login \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "username": "testuser",
    "password": "testpass",
    "numModules": 2
  }'
```

## Validation Rules:

Your `LoginRequest` also has validation annotations:

```java
@NotBlank(message = "Username is required")
@Size(min = 1, max = 50, message = "Username must be between 1 and 50 characters")
private String username;

@NotBlank(message = "Password is required") 
@Size(min = 1, max = 100, message = "Password must be between 1 and 100 characters")
private String password;

@Min(value = 1, message = "Number of modules must be at least 1")
private int numModules;
```

**So your test JSON must:**
- ✅ Have non-empty `username` (1-50 chars)
- ✅ Have non-empty `password` (1-100 chars) 
- ✅ Have `numModules` ≥ 1

## Expected Response:

### **Success (200 OK):**
```json
{
  "success": true,
  "message": "Login successful",
  "session_id": "very-long-random-session-id-string",
  "num_modules": 2
}
```

### **Validation Error (400 Bad Request):**
```json
{
  "timestamp": "2025-11-18T09:57:09.402+00:00",
  "status": 400,
  "error": "Bad Request", 
  "message": "Username is required"
}
```

## Pro Tip: API Documentation

For production apps, you'd typically use **Swagger/OpenAPI** to auto-generate documentation that shows all expected fields:

```java
@PostMapping("/login")
@Operation(summary = "User login", description = "Authenticate user and create session")
public ResponseEntity<?> login(@RequestBody LoginRequest request) {
    // ...
}
```
